   NR-35 (c), in [151.855500860944, 151.855500860945] and [73.818964551323, 73.818964551324], rounded down here)" for

   upper ends; the lower ends are the cuts of NR-35 (c))". numerical audit exchange v932 notes that 151.86 and 73.82 round the
   necessary floors upward and so are not lower endpoints; it CONFIRMS the factors 0.63045, 0.90611, 4.232 and 0.486
   as rounded scalar displays and keeps the ranges HEURISTIC, since their upper figures use an uncertified FLOAT
   moment value.
2. Records, no statement of this file changes: numerical audit exchange v932 (14:51:05Z) CONFIRMS the numerical integration of
   version 27 within its conditional scope (the 24 cut intervals, 20 moment-threshold intervals and 60 trial
   decisions against numerical audit exchange v921 and v923; the rates and price rooms against v926), with the same curvature erratum as
   AT2908, corrected in version 28. numerical audit exchange v931 (14:48:16Z) CONFIRMS the numerical integration of the theory auditor's
   SOURCE013_CHAIN_CONDITIONS_v05. The theory auditor's final theory handoff (reports/FINAL_THEORY_AUDIT_v01.md of agent the theory auditor;
   audit_v883, 14:51:44Z) is CONFIRMED for its current-tuple arithmetic (AT2910) and as a faithful report of the
   mathematical scope through AT2909 (AT2911); that the completed numerical premises and positive scalar rooms prove

3. Header and the completion line. Nothing else changes; section S keeps the cutoff of version 27 (the theory auditor audit_v880,

Change log of version 28 against version 27.
1. S.5 item 3, the summary of the second theory agent's Lemma E (T2_FS1_EPS_v01): "by concavity of 1 - (1 + u)^(-q/2)" for "by
   convexity of 1 - (1 + u)^(-q/2)". The theory auditor REFUTES the word (AT2908; audit_v882): for q > 0 the function 1 - (1 +
   u)^(-q/2) is concave on u >= 0, with second derivative -(q/2)(q/2 + 1)(1 + u)^(-q/2 - 2), equal to -861/400 at u =
   0 for q = 21/10, and concavity gives 0 <= 1 - (1 + u)^(-q/2) <= (q/2) u. The correction changes neither Lemma E
   nor its coefficient q/2, the formula eps = 2t + t^2 or the dilation law (AT2876, AT2877).
2. Status of NR-35 and status line of NR-34 Remark 8: the theory auditor's audit of version 27 CONFIRMS NR-35 (a) under all its
   imported hypotheses (AT2905) and NR-35 (b), (c) with Remark 8 of NR-34, by an independent 1152-bit evaluation of
   all 24 cut intervals at the old and current rates, all 20 threshold intervals and all 60 trial decisions (AT2906);
   version 27 is CONFIRMED as a faithful integration apart from the word of item 1 (AT2907); that the enlarged near
   budget proves the complete fixed-parameter stationary theorem is OPEN (AT2909). The theory auditor also CONFIRMS the scaling
   factors and the rough brackets of S.3 (prompt section 14) as rounded HEURISTIC displays, not certified upper
   endpoints (audit_v882).
3. Header and the completion line. Nothing else changes; section S keeps the cutoff of version 27 (the theory auditor audit_v880,
   AT2900; numerical audit exchange v926). Later records (the theory auditor audit_v881, AT2901 to AT2904, the dependency account
   SOURCE013_CHAIN_CONDITIONS_v05; numerical audit exchange v927 to v930) change no statement of this file and are summarized
   in theory_v24.

   (theory_v21 V21-1 (d)); PROVED (AT2437). The Corollary records the exact free-gain threshold

   exchange v725; AT2456 to AT2458; AT2459 OPEN).

   R, then u_3 <= R mu_r^2/(phi K), and with u_3 >= L the cut satisfies mu_r >= (phi K L/R)^(1/2) (PROVED: AT2439,
   AT2464). Corollary (a): at the plan of record and the whole-plane rate the least cut lies in [138.282830152122,
   138.282830152123] at the (R4) version 2 tuple (AT2496) and in [140.055012371285, 140.055012371286] at the version
   1 tuple (AT2465, AT2510, AT2542). Corollary (b): with the T_IS coefficient q of AT-362, K_q = c_tr' + q, it is

   ||Delta S(0)||_op >= 1899576001577/10^12 give u_3 >= 3799152003154/10^12, and then the least cut is
   [266.304848871307, 266.304848871308] with K and [223.601268661262, 223.601268661263] with K_q (AT2566). Corollary
   (d): the same identity lets Lemma HI' use the T_IS coefficient q/2, K_half = c_tr' + q/2 (AT2565), and then the
   least cut is [207.422143510389, 207.422143510390] (AT2566). All values were computed by

Change log of version 21 against version 20.
1. NR-22, Use (AT2333 CONFIRMED, AT2334 REFUTED; audit_v715). The "near test" there is the cap-level form
   e_HF <= c + 1 - q - b0 of theory_v10 V10-4 (a). In the H4 join the principal part gives -beta_* ||g||^2, and the
   near loss and every other full-L^2 loss eps_rest are paid from it, so the H4 step at rate b0 needs
   e_HF + eps_rest <= beta_* - b0 with a certified whole-plane matrix rate beta_* (AT2333). The cap-level form is the
   special case beta_* = c + 1 - q, the largest rate allowed (AT-211); the claim that it supplies the H4 rate with no
   background dependence except through D is REFUTED (AT2334; exact countermodel beta_* = 3/5, b0 = 11/20,
   e = 77/500). The Use paragraph now says which form the FLOAT tables passed. NR-22 itself, a bound of the near
   form alone, is unchanged.
2. NR-27 item 2: the near window at b0 0.55 and 0.60 is a window of the cap-level form; the same qualification.
3. Section S, the rate (S.0 item 4, S.2, S.3 (c) rate and closure margin, S.4 row G3, S.5 item 3). Under H4-COMP the
   printed NEAR_CHECK_v02 near values (theory_agent_1/research/closure_check/; stipulated uncertified inputs) need beta_* >=
   0.704017099351 at (b0, mu) = (11/20, 72) and 0.696851660742 at (103/200, 68) with eps_rest = 0 (AT2335 CONFIRMED).
   The room "about 0.05 for the matrix target 3/5" of theory_v15 V15-6 (b) item 1, quoted in S.3, was a reading-(i)
   value and is withdrawn (theory_v20 erratum E-v19-4). Both auditors certify the numerical auditor's exact recounts of the admitted

   with the minimum at the origin root 000 (AT2372, AT2373, AT2384 to AT2386; numerical audit exchange v700, v706), and
   M >= (761272835221/10^12) I on r^2 >= 49/15 (AT2400 to AT2402; numerical audit exchange v717). The gap (61/67, 49/15) is not

Change log of version 20 against version 19.
1. S.3 (b), R6.4 line (AT2248 REFUTED the decimals 0.0617041, 0.3085204 and 0.3702245 as necessary bounds; they lie
   above the exact thresholds). The condition is now stated with the exact thresholds alpha/(2c - 2) at the binary64
   c, 2c - 2 = 1824676646553933/1125899906842624, and the printed decimals are labelled sufficient choices. The other
   decimal abbreviations of S.3 (b) and (c) are marked approximate; every condition is the exact expression beside it.
2. S.3 (c), interpolation (AT2249 REFUTED "an exact identity"): Hoelder interpolation in Fourier space is the
   inequality N_q(f) <= N_2(f)^(3 - q) N_3(f)^(q - 2) for 2 <= q <= 3, strict for nonzero W f in H^3 when 2 < q < 3;
   the exact identity is the integer-order energy expansion of section 0.
3. S.0 item 3 (AT2250 REFUTED the blanket wording): the prefactors 1/beta_1, 20, 8 and 10/3 of the O1 bounds are
   explicit; the compact-remainder constant C_eps, and with it C_1, is not computed. The conclusion, that O1 alone
   gives no numerical invariant ball, is unchanged.
4. Section S, the dated background inventory (S.0 item 2, S.1 (d), S.2, S.4 row B, S.5 item 2): 87 of the 128 radial
   roots, r^2 in [0, 57/71] union [49/15, infinity), by both auditors (numerical audit exchange v637, AT2240, AT2241); the theory auditor
   found the 86-index text of version 19 correct as a dated cutoff (AT2246). The cutoff of version 20 is AT2251 and
   numerical audit exchange v643.

## S. Part VII summary (new in version 19; corrected in versions 20 to 27)

This section answers Part VII item 2 of the prompt for this file: the new inverse and regularity estimates, the
explicit constants, the optimized parameters, the invariant-ball conditions, the remaining open lemmas, where the
proofs are, and the dependencies on the established reference lemmas R1 to R11. It summarizes NR-1 to NR-34 below and
adds no new result: no status changes here. A result is PROVED only where a the theory auditor CONFIRMED entry is cited, and a
number is certified only where the numerical auditor and the theory auditor CONFIRM it. Constants that come from other agents are marked as such;
they are not results of this file. The established results (Part 0 and Part II of the prompt, R1 to R11) are used and
not re-proved.

Freeze. This section states the certified state at the cutoff of version 27: the theory auditor audit_v880 (AT2900, 14:28:22Z) and
numerical audit exchange v926 (14:31:48Z). Later work is pending and is not counted here; the theory exchange (theory_v23 and
later) records it.

Setting of the closure route. Chain center C2-N1-013 (Candidate 007 with the exact correction b18s2; coordinator
decision 2026-11-03T11:25Z). c = c_l = 1.810319210, in computations the binary64 value
4076476460239181/2251799813685248; c_l is not varied and Candidate 007 is unchanged. Route Q with the CE variant
(HF-Q-CE) at P-S1 = (q, sigma, r0) = (21/10, 1, 1/2), W = (1 + 4|X|^2)^(1/2), norm N(f) = ||<D>^q (W f)||.

### S.0 Answer in brief

1. O1, the k = 0 regularity lifting, is PROVED as a conditional theorem for the full variable-coefficient operator,
   not a frozen inverse (NR-1, AT-031; steps NR-2, AT-028 to AT-030). For Candidate 007 its hypotheses are certified
   at four tuples, where it applies with the constants of S.1 (c) (P-BG1, AT-056; P-BG2, AT-074; P-BG3, AT-097; P-BG4,
   AT-172; theory_v19 T1.1).
2. For the chain center 013, O1 is PROVED as a conditional application (NR-31, AT-1483, AT-1484). It needs only the
   matrix lower M >= beta I with some beta > 0, a gradient bound G < 2.00215453 and the structural premises RC-2 and
   RC-4 (AT-674, AT-675), not the closure targets. Both field premises now hold on the whole plane: the theory auditor's actual
   review of the numerical auditor's executed complete background union CONFIRMS M >= (704955805507/10^12) I, |grad h| <= 1/4 and
   ||Sym Du||_op <= 137/125 on all of R^2, all 128 radial indices, under the admitted exact-source and
   containing-arithmetic hypotheses (numerical audit exchange v749; AT2534 to AT2536, audit_v771; adopted, numerical audit exchange v755;
   AT2537 OPEN). Hence, under the other retained hypotheses of NR-31 (the generator, semigroup and common-domain
   premises of FW-C2-O1Hq-v1, RC-2, RC-4 and the weighted decay of F_*), L_* k0 = -F_* has a unique L2 solution k0
   with W k0 in H^(21/10), N(k0) <= N(F_*)/beta_1 + C_1 ||F_*||_2 for every 0 < beta_1 < 3/5 with a finite uncomputed
   C_1, and <X> k0 in C^(1,1/10); the theory auditor CONFIRMS this application (AT2541). With beta = 704955805507/10^12 in place
   of 3/5 the same substitution gives every 0 < beta_1 < 704955805507/10^12 (CONFIRMED, AT2599). This is O1 at
   one background; no uniform small-k statement follows.
3. The prefactors of the O1 bounds are explicit: 1/beta_1 at 013 (S.1 (d)) and 20, 8 and 10/3 at P-BG1 to P-BG4 (S.1
   (c)), with the L2 inverse bound 1/(beta_0 - G) at 013. The compact-remainder constant C_eps of T1-Lemma-WG is not
   computed, and with it the constant C_1 of the ||F||_2 term (NR-1; NR-3 item 1; NR-31 Remark 3; AT2250). So O1
   alone gives no numerical invariant ball.
4. No complete existence theorem is certified. The first unresolved condition is O3, the closure inequality of the
   invariant ball (ledger v13, coordinator X12, 2026-11-04T13:39:44Z, not audited at the cutoff; ledger v12, X11,
   2026-11-04T09:59:25Z, named the same condition, and its changed theory and closure blocks are CONFIRMED within
   their scopes, AT2516, AT2517; AT2519 OPEN). In the H4 step the near loss is paid from the whole-plane matrix rate:
   e_HF + eps_rest <= beta_* - b0 (AT2333; AT2334 REFUTES the cap-level reading). The whole-plane lower beta_* =
   704955805507/10^12 is now certified (item 2), and so are the certified whole-plane uppers of D4, D5 and D6 of a_n
   (S.5 item 3; AT2869 to AT2871, audit_v872; adopted in numerical audit exchange v917, AT2885). With them and the certified
   (R4) version 2 inputs (S.2) the printed near upper at the plan of record (b0, mu) = (11/20, 72) is e_up =
   154030213826/10^12 (NEAR_CHECK_v08, a conditional scalar check, CONFIRMED by the theory auditor's independent 1152-bit
   evaluation, AT2887, audit_v877, AT2889 OPEN, and by the numerical auditor's independent 768-bit recomputation, the numerical audit exchange
   v920), so every full-L^2 rest loss of the H4 join must fit in eps_rest <= 925591681/10^12 = 9.25591681e-4 at the
   plan of record (8091034531/10^12 at the fallback (103/200, 68), whose mu 68 kernel constants and allowance are
   UNAUDITED). At the plan of record eps_rest is the only input of this rate test without a certified value; the test
   keeps the localization hypotheses of the near check and the Fourier hypotheses of NR-25. With the direct moment
   bound of NR-35 (PROVED; AT2890, AT2891, audit_v878) e_up falls to 152627111489/10^12 and the rooms grow to
   1164347009/(5*10^11) = 2.328694018e-3 and 1970913581/(2*10^11) = 9.854567905e-3 (NEAR_CHECK_v09). The second theory agent's closure
   checks version 7 (theory_agent_2/notes/T2_CLOSURE_CHECK_v07.md) and version 8 (T2_CLOSURE_CHECK_v08.md) bind NEAR_CHECK_v08
   and NEAR_CHECK_v09, have these rate rooms as E2 margins and the exact price rooms 9941475955 (plan) and
   47277952671/5 (fallback) for C_rest, the values of version 6 (printed as the lower displays 9941475900 and
   9455590500), and the theory auditor CONFIRMS both as conditional scalar calculations (AT2894 to AT2896, audit_v879; adopted,
   numerical audit exchange v926); their verdict is CLOSURE OPEN, with eps_rest and C_rest as zero stand-ins and the block-H
   hypotheses OPEN (AT2897). Three sufficient estimates of the rest are excluded at stated budgets: the unsplit
   TIS-FG bound against the free gain (NR-33 Corollary (c) and Remark 4, M_S >= 10529.58564315332; AT2456 to AT2458,

### S.1 New inverse and regularity estimates

(a) NR-1, T1-Thm-O1Hq (PROVED as a conditional theorem, AT-031). For L h = V.grad h - h + grad rho_bar.U[h] under
(BG-reg; gamma_*), (B-L2) with beta_0 = c + 1 - theta - kappa > 0 (R1), (BG-q; q, sigma, r0, beta):
lambda_max N_q(X) <= -beta on R^2, and 2 < q < 3, 0 <= sigma < 1 + gamma_*, r0 > 0, the unique L2 solution of
L h0 = -F (R2) satisfies

    ||<D>^q (W h0)|| <= (1/beta_1) ||<D>^q (W F)|| + C_1 ||F||   for 0 < beta_1 < min(beta, beta_0),
    C_1 = beta_1^(-1) (C_(beta - beta_1)/(beta_0 - beta_1))^(1/2),

and <X>^sigma h0 is in C^(1,alpha) for 0 < alpha <= q - 2, with norm at most c_mu C_S(q, alpha) times the right side
(T1.2 of the theory exchange). The proof conjugates by W, proves the weighted Riesz commutator compact (Lemma E), a
weighted Garding inequality with rate beta - epsilon (Lemma WG) and the identification of the weighted semigroup with
the L2 semigroup (Lemma SG), then integrates the semigroup (NR-2).

(b) T1-Cor-O1R9 (PROVED as a conditional corollary in the corrected form of AT-032). A heavier exterior weight
sigma_9 > sigma, through R9.4 at weight sigma_9 with mu_infty = 1 + c(sigma_9 - 1 - alpha) > 0, a finite-radius
margin mu_R > 0 and the absorption of the nonlocal coefficient: H_(sigma_9,alpha)(h0) <= C_R [ ||<X>^(sigma_9)
F||_(C^(1,alpha)(|X| > R)) + C_(W^(-1),2R) C_S N(h0) + ||h0||_2 ], C_R not explicit (NR-1). For 007 at sigma_9 = 7/4
the source condition is supplied by AT-004, conditional on AN-001; mu_R > 0 is FLOAT only.

(c) Activations for Candidate 007 (theory_v19 T1.1; each BG-3 cover certified by the numerical auditor). With N the norm of the tuple:
- P-BG1 = (13/5, 7/5, 1/4, 1/10): N(h0) <= 20 N(F) + 20 (20 C_(1/20)/39)^(1/2) ||F||_2, and <X>^(7/5) h0 in
  C^(1,alpha) for alpha <= 3/5 (AT-056; AN-022, 230 leaves).
- P-BG2 = (5/2, 7/5, 1/2, 1/4): N(h0) <= 8 N(F) + 8 (8 C_(1/8)/15)^(1/2) ||F||_2, alpha <= 1/2 (AT-074). Restricted
  uniform family: N(k) <= 1/(16 D) gives beta_k >= 1/16 and a uniform full weighted inverse.
- P-BG3 = (21/10, 1, 1/2, 3/5) and P-BG4 = (21/10, 7/5, 1, 3/5): N(h0) <= (10/3) N(F) + (10/3) (10 C_(3/10)/17)^(1/2)
  ||F||_2, alpha <= 1/10 (AT-097, AT-172). Every real S1 k with N(k) <= 3/(20 D) gives a full weighted inverse of
  L + U[k].grad on weighted H^(21/10) with a common operator bound and restricted gap beta_k >= 3/20 (AT-097; the
  same at P-BG4, AT-172). D is symbolic, so this is not a self-map theorem.
- The forward Duhamel identity h0(X) = int_0^infinity e^(-s) g(Phi_s X) ds, g = F + grad rho_bar.U[h0] (AT-080).

(d) NR-31, T1-Prop-O1-013 (PROVED as a conditional application, AT-1483, AT-1484). At 013 and P-S1, with
M(X) = (c + 1 + sigma V.X/(eta + |X|^2)) I - q Sym DV + A, eta = 1/4, assume (H-a) M >= beta I on R^2 for a rational
beta > 0, (H-b) sup |grad h| <= G for a certified rational G < 2.00215453 (beta_0 > 2.00215453, AN-010), and (H-c)
RC-2 and RC-4 (AT-674, AT-675). Then L_* k0 = -F_* has a unique L2 solution, the L2 inverse has norm at most
1/(beta_0 - G) (Gronwall and the Neumann argument of RC-3, AT-060, AT-123),

    N(k0) <= N(F_*)/beta_1 + C_1 ||F_*||_2   for 0 < beta_1 < min(beta, beta_0 - G),
    C_1 = beta_1^(-1) (C_(beta - beta_1)/(beta_0 - G - beta_1))^(1/2),

and <X> k0 is in C^(1,1/10). With the certified source values N(F_*) <= 0.0055546 (AT-1430, AT-1431) and ||F_*||_2 <=
7.961206656880281e-7 (AT-1413) and the whole-plane field premises beta = 704955805507/10^12 and G = 1/4 (AT2534 to
AT2536), the L2 inverse bound is 1/(beta_0 - 1/4) < 1/1.75215453 < 0.570726, and the estimate holds for every 0 <
beta_1 < 704955805507/10^12 (AT2599; for 0 < beta_1 < 3/5 already AT2541); C_1 stays symbolic, and the statement is
conditional on the retained analytic hypotheses of NR-31. The strain bound and the target 1/4 are not premises of
NR-31; 1/4 is needed for Lemma KB in the closure (NR-31 Remark 1).

(e) Sharpness and necessary conditions for inverse estimates.
- NR-7, T1-Thm-HFNEC (PROVED, AT-211; integration AT-300). If a k = 0 weak inequality E(g) <= -b ||g||^2 + C0
  ||W^(-1) J^(-q) g||^2 holds, then b <= beta, the BG-q margin. So {b : C^opt(b) < infinity} lies between (-infinity,
  beta) and (-infinity, beta]; the endpoint is not decided. Corollaries: for every normalized background in the class
  of AT-167, beta <= c + 1 - q - max(q(2c - 2) - a_0, 0) <= c + 1 - q (EXACT; AT-087, AT-167, AT-266), and 3/5 <=
  beta < 0.6588409179 for 007 at P-S2 (AN-056, AT-172; AN-068, AT-199).
- NR-3 item 6 (PROVED: AT2713, AT2714; AT2715 OPEN): for the zero background, which is not normalized, the L2 rate is
  c + 1, yet the unique L2 solution for a smooth annular S1 forcing is not in H^q near the origin for q >= 1 + 1/c
  (about 1.5523887). So no H^q estimate with q >= 1 + 1/c follows from an L2 gap and smooth forcing alone over a
  class containing this background (the scope of audit_v821; it does not classify sufficient hypotheses). In NR-1 the
  origin hypothesis is part of (BG-q), which holds on all of R^2.
- NR-6 (q-uniform ceiling of the coarse criteria; T1-Prop-Nstar PROVED, AT-095, AT-118): the coarse absorption
  criteria need N_(q,sigma,r0)(F) < N_*(q) with sup_(2 < q < c + 1) N_*(q) <= 1.177171 and N_*(21/10) <= 1.097831.
  T1-Rem-LK0 is PROVED in the qualified form of AT-094; T1-Prop-LKmodel is PROVED for the model, its transfer to the
  full operator OPEN (AT-094).
- NR-8 (PROVED, AT-212): frozen-symbol monotonicity LF-1 at points with s(X) <= c and the pointwise form
  inequalities FZ used by the weak-energy tests.

(f) Tools for the closure charges (PROVED; NR-21, NR-22, NR-26 and NR-27 PROVED conditionally, NR-19 under its
premises, NR-13 as a necessary condition of the conditional test, NR-34 as a necessary condition for the Lemma HI'
upper to fit; used to price the near and far terms, not inverse estimates themselves): NR-16 (AT-342), NR-17 (AT-487,
AT-488), NR-21 (AT-664, AT-706, AT-756), NR-22 (AT-691, AT-757), NR-23 (AT-845), NR-24 (AT-886, AT-887), NR-25
(AT-890 to AT-892), NR-26 (AT-911), NR-27 (AT-1094, AT-1134), NR-28 (AT-1091 to AT-1093, AT-1134), NR-29 (AT-1241,
AT-1250), NR-30 (AT-1328), NR-32 (AT-1512, AT-1513), NR-33 (AT2437), NR-34 (AT2439, AT2464), and the rules NR-9 to
NR-15, NR-18 to NR-20.

### S.3 Optimized parameters (prompt section 14)

(a) Which parameters are free. The start values alpha = 0.5, delta = 0.4, sigma = 1.75 of section 14 belong to the
frozen route (R6.4: Hoelder exponent alpha and frozen exponent delta with alpha/(2c - 2) < delta < 1, 1 < nu < 2)
and to the exterior route (R9 at sigma = 7/4). This file proves O1 in a weighted Sobolev route (NR-1), whose free
parameters are (q, sigma, r0) of N and the BG-q rate beta; the Hoelder exponent is the output alpha <= q - 2 of the
embedding. No frozen inverse enters NR-1 or NR-31, so delta does not enter them; the condition on delta binds only a
stronger Hoelder lifting through R6.4.

(b) Constraints (EXACT or PROVED unless marked).
- q < c + 1 and beta <= c + 1 - q (the origin cap; NR-3, NR-7 (a)); beta is also the rate ceiling of every weak
  energy inequality at that norm (NR-7), so the rate b0 of the closure test satisfies b0 <= c + 1 - q.
- q > 2 for the C^1 embedding, and every q >= 1 + 1/c (about 1.5523888) needs an origin hypothesis beyond the L2 gap
  (NR-3 item 6, PROVED in its audited scope: AT2713, AT2714).
- 0 <= sigma < 1 + gamma_* (Lemma E, NR-2); for 007 gamma_* = 1 - 1/c, so sigma < 2 - 1/c (about 1.4476112310);
  at a corrected center sigma < 1 + min(gamma_*, gamma_h) (AT-123).
- sigma < 4 - 4/c (about 1.7904449) for W F in H^q (moment condition of AT-012; every finite q, AT-048).
- A globally binding origin needs sigma >= q(1 - 1/c), about 0.9399836 at q = 21/10 (NR-5.4; EXACT necessary
  condition, AT-070, AT-078; not sufficient).
- R6.4 (only for a stronger Hoelder lifting): alpha/(2c - 2) < delta < 1, with 2c - 2 =
  1824676646553933/1125899906842624 (EXACT at the binary64 c; about 1.6206384204). The exact thresholds are
  562949953421312/9123383232769665 (about 0.0617040783) at alpha = 1/10, 562949953421312/1824676646553933 (about
  0.3085203915) at alpha = 1/2 and 1125899906842624/3041127744256555 (about 0.3702244698) at alpha = 3/5. The
  decimals 0.0617041, 0.3085204 and 0.3702245 lie above them, so delta >= each decimal is a sufficient choice, not a
  necessary bound (AT2248). The start value delta = 0.4 is admissible at alpha = 1/2; any delta < 1 needs alpha <
  2c - 2. sigma = 1 cannot be inserted as nu = 1 in R6.4, which needs 1 < nu < 2 (AT-087).
- R9.4 at sigma_9 = 7/4 (T1-Cor-O1R9): mu_infty = 1 + c(3/4 - alpha) = 1.4525798025... at alpha = 1/2 and
  2.1767074866... at alpha = 1/10 (EXACT arithmetic at the binary64 c), both positive; mu_R > 0 is FLOAT only for 007
  and not evaluated for 013.
- NR-6.4 (CLAIMED): BG-L4 (R9 exterior margins) needs sigma > 1 + alpha - 1/c (about 0.5476, 0.6976, 0.9476 at
  alpha = 1/10, 1/4, 1/2), and BG-L1 (R6.3, R6.4) holds for 007 for alpha < (a0 + 1)/b - 1 (about 3.3165) with the
  EXACT a0.
So the start value sigma = 1.75 is not admissible for the Sobolev route at 007 (it exceeds 2 - 1/c); it is admissible
as the exterior weight sigma_9 of T1-Cor-O1R9.

map T(k) = -A_k^(-1) F_* (NR-4; the second theory agent's framework). Theorem HF-Q, Theorem CE and Corollary HF-Q-CE of the second theory agent are
CONFIRMED conditionally (AT-227 to AT-230, AT-234 to AT-238, AT-243, AT-272 to AT-275, AT-283), with the transfer to
P-S1 (AT-303 to AT-308), under the retained bridge hypotheses. The weak fixed-point step is R5. The ball would be
invariant, and the route would give a stationary solution, once the ledger items are certified:

| G3 (H4) | near and far charges within the allowance D, and the near rate e_HF + eps_rest <= beta_* - b0 (AT2333) | OPEN: the whole-plane uppers of D4, D5 and D6 of a_n are certified (D6(a_n) < 63050.392730580225839237053713; AT2869 to AT2871, audit_v872; adopted, numerical audit exchange v917, AT2885), (R4) is certified (Proposition R4, versions 1 and 2; AT2477 to AT2479, AT2520 to AT2522) and beta_* is certified on the whole plane (row B); at these inputs the near (R1) test passes at the plan of record with e_up = 154030213826/10^12, and the plan of record needs eps_rest <= 925591681/10^12 (NEAR_CHECK_v08, a conditional scalar check, AT2887; the one-variable D6(a_n) thresholds stay 64623.4 and 63279.8), or eps_rest <= 1164347009/(5*10^11) with NR-35 (NEAR_CHECK_v09; AT2890, AT2891); the remainder pieces of H4-R have no 013 bound (H4-REST), and three sufficient estimates of them are excluded at stated budgets (NR-33, NR-34; Corollary O of the second theory agent, AT2583); far needs the remaining analytic far premises (S.5 item 4) |

## 0. Notation

- c = c_l, b = 2c - 1, d = b - 1, gamma_9 = 1 - 1/c. For 007: c = 1.8103192102, b = 2.6206384204, gamma_9 = 0.4476112310.
- <X> = (1 + |X|^2)^(1/2). <D> = (1 - Delta)^(1/2), the Fourier multiplier <xi>. ||.|| is the full-plane L2 norm.
  H^s is the Sobolev space with norm ||<D>^s f||.
- R_j: multiplier i xi_j/|xi|. U = (-R1 R2, R1^2), so U[h] has symbol (xi1 xi2/|xi|^2, -xi1^2/|xi|^2).
  Rt_j := d_j <D>^(-1), multiplier i xi_j/<xi> (smooth, no singularity at xi = 0).
- Background: rho_bar is S1 and smooth, u_bar = U[rho_bar], V = c X + u_bar. The linear operator is
  L h = V.grad h - h + grad rho_bar . U[h].
- Weight: W(X) = (1 + |X|^2/r0^2)^(sigma/2), with r0 > 0 and sigma >= 0. Its log-derivative is
  grad log W = sigma X/(r0^2 + |X|^2), and a_W := V.grad log W = sigma V.X/(r0^2 + |X|^2).
- Kmat(X) := [[-d_y rho_bar, d_x rho_bar/2], [d_x rho_bar/2, 0]]. Its largest eigenvalue is
  theta(X) = (|grad rho_bar| - d_y rho_bar)/2, the pointwise quantity of R1.
- N_q(X) := q Sym DV(X) + Kmat(X) - (c + 1 + a_W(X)) I, a symmetric 2 x 2 matrix field.
- Norm (registered in theory_v01 as NORM-T-HqW-v1): N_{q,sigma,r0}(f) := ||<D>^q (W f)||.
  A computable upper bound for 2 <= q <= 3, by Hoelder's inequality in Fourier space:
  N_{q,sigma,r0}(f) <= ||<D>^2(Wf)||^(3-q) ||<D>^3(Wf)||^(q-2), where ||<D>^m g||^2 = sum_{j=0..m} C(m,j) ||D^j g||^2
  with ||D^j g|| the ordered-tuple Frobenius L2 norm (WORKSPACE_CONTRACTS.md convention). This identity is exact, because
  |xi|^(2j) = sum over ordered j-tuples of xi_(i1)^2 ... xi_(ij)^2.

## NR-1. T1-Thm-O1Hq (PROVED as a conditional theorem, AT-031)

Hypotheses.
- (BG-reg; gamma_*) rho_bar and u_bar are C^infinity. For some gamma_* in (0, 1] and every multi-index j,
  |D^j grad rho_bar(X)| + |D^j D u_bar(X)| <= C_j <X>^(-gamma_* - |j|) on R^2.
  For 007 with gamma_* = gamma_9: numerical_audit/research/background_regularity/PROOF.md states |D^q rho| + |D^q u| =
  O(r^(1/c - q)) for every integer q, which with smoothness on compact sets is (BG-reg; gamma_9). Independently
  CONFIRMED as AT-013 (qualitative, constants C_j unspecified). Only finitely many C_j enter each step below. This makes V globally Lipschitz, and |u_bar(X)| <= C <X>^(1 - gamma_*) since
  u_bar(0) = 0 by symmetry.
- (B-L2) beta_0 := c + 1 - theta - kappa > 0 (R1.1; theta = sup_X theta(X), kappa = Hilbert-Schmidt norm of the
  double commutator of [L2] 20-21), together with the remaining hypotheses of R1 and R2 (IMPORTED;
  sources in EXISTING_PROOFS_REFERENCE.md, R1 and R2). The L2 semigroup then obeys ||e^(tL)|| <= e^(-beta_0 t)
  ([L2] eq (7) with the variable rate of [LRT]). For 007, beta_0 > 2.00215453 (HISTORICAL; certified by the numerical auditor as
  AN-010).
- (BG-q; q, sigma, r0, beta) lambda_max(N_q(X)) <= -beta for every X in R^2, with beta > 0.
- Parameters: 2 < q < 3, 0 <= sigma < 1 + gamma_*, r0 > 0.
- Source: F in L2, S1, with W F in H^q.
  For 007, N_{q,sigma,r0}(F) < infinity holds for sigma < 4 - 4/c = 1.7904449 (moment condition of AT-012; every
  finite q, AT-048). F is
  smooth and |D^j F| = O(<X>^(4/c - 5 - j)) by the exact sector structure (AN-001).

Conclusion. Let h0 be the unique L2 solution of L h0 = -F (R2; h0 = integral_0^infinity e^(tL) F dt). Then W h0 is
in H^q. For every beta_1 with 0 < beta_1 < min(beta, beta_0) there is a constant C_1 < infinity, depending on
beta_1, q, sigma, r0 and the background but not on F, such that

  ||<D>^q (W h0)|| <= (1/beta_1) ||<D>^q (W F)|| + C_1 ||F||.                                     (O1-Hq)

Consequently, for 0 < alpha <= q - 2, h0 is in C^(1,alpha) and

  H_{sigma,alpha}(h0) = ||<X>^sigma h0||_(C^(1,alpha)) <= c_mu C_S [ (1/beta_1) N_{q,sigma,r0}(F) + C_1 ||F|| ],

where C_S = C_S(q, alpha) is the Sobolev embedding constant of H^q into C^(1,alpha) (explicit by Fourier inversion)
and c_mu is the C^(1,alpha) multiplier norm of mu = <X>^sigma/W, which lies in C_b^infinity. For r0 <= 1,
r0^sigma <= mu <= 1.

Status: PROVED as a conditional theorem (AT-031; steps AT-028 to AT-030). The conditions are (BG-reg), (B-L2),
(BG-q) and the parameter ranges. The constant is C_1 = beta_1^(-1) (C_(beta - beta_1)/(beta_0 - beta_1))^(1/2), with
C_eps from T1-Lemma-WG; it is finite and not explicit (NR-3 item 1). So the theorem gives a finite A_F for O1 but no
numerical threshold. The S1 symmetry of h0 and ell(h0) = 0 follow from R3 (IMPORTED) with a0 > 0 and F_y(0) = 0.

Activation for Candidate 007 (AT-056, CONFIRMED). At P-BG1 = (q, sigma, r0, beta) = (13/5, 7/5, 1/4, 1/10), the
premises are discharged by AT-013 (BG-reg; gamma_9), AN-010 (B-L2, beta_0 > 2.00215453), AT-048 (W F in H^q, since
sigma < 4 - 4/c) and AN-022 (the global matrix cover, 230 leaves). With beta_0 = 2 and beta_1 = 1/20:
  N(h0) <= 20 N(F) + 20 (20 C_(1/20)/39)^(1/2) ||F||_2,
and <X>^(7/5) h0 is in C^(1,alpha) for 0 < alpha <= 3/5, with ell(h0) = 0. C_(1/20) is finite and uncomputed. This is
the full variable operator, not a frozen inverse. It is a k = 0 statement and gives no invariant ball.

T1-Cor-O1R9 (PROVED as a conditional corollary in the corrected form of AT-032; theory_audit/research/o1_corollary_scope/
PROOF.md). Assume the hypotheses of T1-Thm-O1Hq. Let sigma_9 > sigma with mu_infty(sigma_9, alpha) =
1 + c(sigma_9 - 1 - alpha) > 0, and assume, for k = 0, all hypotheses of R9.4 at weight sigma_9 (PROMPT R9, IMPORTED,
prompt statement only): the envelopes, the exterior outward flow, a positive finite-radius margin
mu_R = q_0 - (1 + alpha) L_R > 0 together with the required absorption of the nonlocal coefficient. Assume also
||<X>^(sigma_9) F||_(C^(1,alpha)(|X|>R)) < infinity. Then
  H_{sigma_9,alpha}(h0) <= C_R [ ||<X>^(sigma_9) F||_(C^(1,alpha)(|X|>R)) + C_(W^(-1),2R) C_S N_{q,sigma,r0}(h0) + ||h0||_2 ],
where C_(W^(-1),2R) is the finite C^(1,alpha) multiplier norm of W^(-1) on B_2R (it can be absorbed into C_R). C_R is
not explicit in R9.4. The source condition is not implied by N_{q,sigma,r0}(F) < infinity (AT-032 gives a separated S1
bump construction); for 007 at sigma_9 = 7/4 it is supplied by AT-004, conditional on AN-001. A positive limiting
margin mu_infty is not a finite-radius certificate.

## NR-2. Steps of the proof (each PROVED: AT-028, AT-029, AT-030)

The proof text below is the version 1 text audited in AT-028 to AT-031. Only the wording repairs of the change log
(item 3) are applied; they are marked "[v2 repair]".

### Step 0. Conjugation (exact)

For u = W h, put L_W u := W L(W^(-1) u). Since W(V.grad(W^(-1) u)) = V.grad u - a_W u,

  L_W u = V.grad u - (1 + a_W) u + B u + E_W u,   B u := grad rho_bar . U[u],
  E_W u := grad rho_bar . (W U[W^(-1) u] - U[u]) = grad rho_bar . [W, U] W^(-1) u.

Properties of a_W under (BG-reg): a_W - sigma c = -sigma c r0^2/(r0^2 + |X|^2) + sigma u_bar.X/(r0^2 + |X|^2) tends to 0
at infinity like <X>^(-gamma_*), and every derivative of a_W of order j >= 1 is O(<X>^(-gamma_* - j)), so
grad a_W is in C_0 with bounded derivatives of all orders.

### T1-Lemma-E (weighted Riesz commutator). PROVED (AT-028)

Assume (BG-reg; gamma_*) and 0 <= sigma < 1 + gamma_*. Then E_W is compact on H^m for m = 0, 1, 2, 3, and hence on H^s
for every s in [0, 3].

Proof.
(a) Kernel. U_1 = -R1 R2 and U_2 = R1^2 are c_i I + T_i, where c_1 = 0, c_2 = -1/2 and T_i is the principal-value
convolution with a kernel k_i that is homogeneous of degree -2, smooth on the unit circle, with mean zero and
|k_i(z)| <= C_k |z|^(-2). The identity part commutes with W. Hence E_W has the kernel
  e(x, y) = grad rho_bar(x) . k(x - y) (W(x)/W(y) - 1).
Two elementary weight bounds: (i) if |x - y| <= <x>/2, then |W(x)/W(y) - 1| <= C_W |x - y|/<x>, with C_W depending
on sigma and r0 (mean value theorem, since |grad log W| <= C <X>^(-1) and <y> is comparable to <x>); (ii) otherwise,
|W(x)/W(y) - 1| <= 1 + W(x)/W(y). Write e = e_near + e_far according to |x - y| <= <x>/2 or not.
- e_near: |e_near(x, y)| <= C <x>^(-gamma_* - 1) |x - y|^(-1) 1{|x - y| <= <x>/2}. Schur's test gives an L2 bound. Cut
  at |x| = N: the part with |x| > N has Schur norm O(N^(-gamma_*)). The part with |x| <= N has a weakly singular
  kernel on a bounded set (|y| <= |x| + <x>/2). Its restriction to |x - y| < epsilon has Schur norm O(epsilon), and
  the rest is Hilbert-Schmidt. So e_near is a norm limit of compact operators, hence compact.
- e_far: |e_far(x, y)| <= C <x>^(-gamma_*) |x - y|^(-2) (1 + W(x)/W(y)) 1{|x - y| > <x>/2}. Its Hilbert-Schmidt norm
  is finite. The "1" part gives integral <x>^(-2 gamma_*) <x>^(-2) dx < infinity. For the W(x)/W(y) part, the y-integral
  of |x - y|^(-4) W(y)^(-2) over |x - y| > <x>/2 is O(<x>^(-4) m_sigma(<x>) + <x>^(-2 - 2 sigma)), where
  m_sigma(t) = integral_{|y| < t} W(y)^(-2) dy is O(1), O(log t) or O(t^(2 - 2 sigma)) for sigma > 1, = 1, < 1.
  Multiplying by <x>^(-2 gamma_*) W(x)^2, which is comparable to <x>^(2 sigma - 2 gamma_*), the x-integral is finite
  when 2 sigma - 2 gamma_* - 4 < -2 (sigma > 1 case), that is when sigma < 1 + gamma_*. [v2 repair: this condition is
  sufficient for the bound used; no necessity is claimed (AT-028).] The cases sigma <= 1 hold with room.
  So e_far is Hilbert-Schmidt and compact.
(b) Derivatives. Differentiation gives the exact identity (the identity part of U commutes with l_j and W)
  d_j (E_W f) = (d_j grad rho_bar) . [W, U] W^(-1) f + grad rho_bar . W [l_j, U] W^(-1) f + E_W (d_j f),   l_j := d_j log W.
Here W[l_j, U]W^(-1) has kernel k(x - y)(l_j(x) - l_j(y)) W(x)/W(y), with |l_j(x) - l_j(y)| <= C |x - y| <x>^(-2) near
the diagonal and <= C(<x>^(-1) + <y>^(-1)) away from it. The coefficient d_j grad rho_bar decays one order faster.
So both new operators satisfy the bounds of (a) with at least the same exponents. Iterating to order 3 (BG-reg up to
|j| = 3 is used here; repeated differentiation also produces nested commutators [l_i, [l_j, U]], with the same
bounds) gives ||E_W f||_(H^m) <= C ||f||_(H^m), and E_W is compact on H^m, because each term in the
expansion is compact on L2 and is applied to a derivative of f of order at most m.
(c) Interpolation. A linear operator that is compact on H^2 and on H^3 is compact on the complex interpolation space
H^s, 2 < s < 3 (Hilbert couple; Persson's interpolation theorem for compact operators). The same holds for 0 <= s <= 2. QED.
[v2 repair: the audited argument for (c) (AT-028, theory_audit/research/weighted_commutator_audit/PROOF.md) needs no
compact interpolation theorem. Put J_N = chi(X/N) psi(D/N) with smooth cutoffs equal to one near zero. The J_N are
uniformly bounded and converge strongly to the identity on H^0 and H^3, so compactness of E_W at both endpoints gives
||(I - J_N) E_W|| -> 0 on H^0 and on H^3, hence on every H^s, 0 <= s <= 3, by interpolation of bounded operators.
Each J_N is compact on H^s (Rellich). So E_W is a norm limit of compact operators on H^s.]

The constraint sigma < 1 + gamma_* enters only through e_far, that is, through low frequencies far from the origin.
For gamma_* = gamma_9 the ceiling is 1 + gamma_9 = 2 - 1/c = 1.4476112310 (007). Without the decaying coefficient
grad rho_bar, the same commutator is bounded only for sigma < 1 (the A_2 condition for power weights in R^2).

### Standard tools used (not new; cited)

- (K1) If f is in C_0(R^2) and p is in L^infinity with p(xi) -> 0 as |xi| -> infinity, then f(X)p(D) and p(D)f(X) are
  compact on L2. Approximate f and p by compactly supported functions; f(X)p(D) with f, p in L2 is Hilbert-Schmidt.
- (K2) For f in C_0 that is uniformly continuous, [R_j, f] is compact (Uchiyama's CMO theorem). By (K1),
  [Rt_j, f] = R_j [m(D), f] + [R_j, f] m(D) is also compact, since m(xi) = |xi|/<xi> and 1 - m tends to 0 at infinity.
- (K3) Commutator calculus. [v2 repair: (K3) is used, and proved in AT-029, only for coefficients a in the (BG-reg)
  scope: u_bar_k, a_W and d_i rho_bar under (BG-reg; gamma_*).] If grad a is in C_b^infinity (all derivatives bounded), then
  [<D>^q, a] <D>^(-q) = -i sum_j (d_j a)(X) Op(d_(xi_j) <xi>^q) <D>^(-q) + Op(r_a),
  where r_a is in S^(-2)_(1,0), with seminorms bounded by finitely many seminorms of D^2 a (Hoermander symbol calculus).
  If D^2 a is in C_0, Op(r_a) and r_a-type terms of order -1 with C_0 coefficients are compact (Cordes'
  compactness criterion). In particular, for a = u_bar_k (so the vector field is u_bar):
  [<D>^q, u_bar.grad] <D>^(-q) = q sum_(j,k) (d_j u_bar_k) Rt_j^* Rt_k + compact,
  and for the dilation exactly [<D>^q, c X.grad] <D>^(-q) = q c |D|^2 <D>^(-2) = q c sum_j Rt_j^* Rt_j.

### T1-Lemma-WG (weighted Garding inequality). PROVED (AT-029)

Assume (BG-reg; gamma_*), the R1 hypotheses that make kappa finite, (BG-q; q, sigma, r0, beta), 2 < q < 3 and
0 <= sigma < 1 + gamma_*. Then for every epsilon > 0 there is C_eps < infinity such that every S1 Schwartz function u
satisfies

  Re < <D>^q L_W u, <D>^q u > <= -(beta - epsilon) ||<D>^q u||^2 + C_eps ||W^(-1) u||^2.

Proof. Put g = <D>^q u (Schwartz) and LL := <D>^q L_W <D>^(-q). By Step 0 and (K3),
  LL = V.grad + q c sum_j Rt_j^* Rt_j + q sum_(j,k) (d_j u_bar_k) Rt_j^* Rt_k - (1 + a_W) + B + C_1,
where C_1 is the sum of [<D>^q, a_W]<D>^(-q), [<D>^q, d_i rho_bar]<D>^(-q) U_i, <D>^q E_W <D>^(-q> and the remainder
in (K3). All of these are compact on L2: by (K3) with grad a_W and D^2 rho_bar in C_0, and by Lemma E for E_W on H^q.
Further:
- Re<V.grad g, g> = -c ||g||^2, since div V = 2c.
- Re<B g, g> = <P0 g, g> + <K g, g>, where <P0 g, g> = integral (R g)^T Kmat (R g) dX and K = (1/2)[R1,[R1,b]] -
  (1/2)[R1,[R2,a]] is Hilbert-Schmidt. This is R1's exact decomposition ([L2] 74-84), IMPORTED and used here only as an
  identity; it is not re-proved.
- Re<(d_j u_bar_k) Rt_j^* Rt_k g, g> = <(d_j u_bar_k) Rt_k g, Rt_j g> + compact by (K2). Summing gives
  integral (Rt g)^T Sym D u_bar (Rt g) for real g.
Hence Re<LL g, g> = Q(g) + <C_2 g, g>, with C_2 compact and
  Q(g) := q integral (Rt g)^T Sym DV (Rt g) + integral (R g)^T Kmat (R g) - integral (c + 1 + a_W) |g|^2,
where Sym DV = c I + Sym D u_bar.

Frequency split. Fix Lambda >= 1, put P = 1{|xi| > Lambda}(D), and write QQ for the bounded self-adjoint operator of
the form Q. Split QQ = QQ_inf + QQ_1, where QQ_inf = q c sum_j Rt_j^* Rt_j - (c + 1 + sigma c) is a Fourier
multiplier and QQ_1 collects the terms with the coefficients Sym D u_bar, Kmat and a_W - sigma c. Each is in C_0 by
(BG-reg). By (K1), QQ_1 (1 - P) and (1 - P) QQ_1 are compact, and QQ_inf commutes with P. Therefore
  Q(g) = <QQ P g, P g> + <QQ_inf (1 - P) g, (1 - P) g> + <C_3 g, g>,   C_3 compact.
- Low frequencies. <QQ_inf (1-P) g, (1-P) g> = integral_{|xi| <= Lambda} (q c |xi|^2/<xi>^2 - c - 1 - sigma c) |g^(xi)|^2 dxi
  <= (q c - c - 1 - sigma c) ||(1 - P) g||^2. As |X| -> infinity, N_q(X) -> (q c - c - 1 - sigma c) I by (BG-reg),
  so (BG-q) implies q c - c - 1 - sigma c <= -beta.
- High frequencies. Write g_h = P g. Since Rt = m(D) R with 0 <= 1 - m(xi) <= 1/(2|xi|^2), we have
  ||Rt g_h - R g_h|| <= (2 Lambda^2)^(-1) ||g_h||, and the first term of Q(g_h) equals
  q integral (R g_h)^T Sym DV (R g_h) up to an error at most q ||Sym DV||_inf Lambda^(-2) ||g||^2.
  The identity sum_j R_j^* R_j = I gives integral a |g_h|^2 = integral a |R g_h|^2 + sum_j <[R_j, a] g_h, R_j g_h> for
  a = c + 1 + a_W, and [R_j, a] = [R_j, a_W - sigma c] is compact by (K2). Hence
  <QQ g_h, g_h> = integral (R g_h)^T N_q(X) (R g_h) dX + O(Lambda^(-2)) ||g||^2 + compact
              <= -beta ||g_h||^2 + O(Lambda^(-2)) ||g||^2 + compact,
  using the pointwise matrix inequality (BG-q) and sum_j ||R_j g_h||^2 = ||g_h||^2.
Adding the two parts gives Re<LL g, g> <= -beta ||g||^2 + C' Lambda^(-2) ||g||^2 + <C_4 g, g>, with C_4 compact.
Choose Lambda with C' Lambda^(-2) <= epsilon/2.

Compact remainder. Approximate C_4 in operator norm, within epsilon/4, by a finite-rank operator
sum_i <., psi_i> phi_i with Schwartz psi_i and phi_i. Then <g, psi_i> = <W^(-1) u, W <D>^q psi_i> and
W <D>^q psi_i is in L2. So |<C_4 g, g>| <= (epsilon/2) ||g||^2 + C_eps ||W^(-1) u||^2. QED.

Remark. The pointwise matrix N_q enters only at high frequencies. At low frequencies only its limit at infinity
enters; near the origin, low frequencies are compact. The singular Riesz symbol at xi = 0 never meets a
pseudo-differential calculus: Kmat is treated through R1's exact identity, and the transport commutators use the
smooth multipliers Rt_j.

### T1-Lemma-SG (semigroup and identification; was "Lemma S"). PROVED (AT-030)

Assume (BG-reg; gamma_*) and 0 <= sigma < 1 + gamma_*.
(i) L_W generates a C_0 semigroup on the S1 part of H^q, 0 <= q <= 3, and S1 Schwartz functions form a core.
(ii) For u0 in the generator domain D(L_W), W^(-1) e^(t L_W) u0 = e^(tL) (W^(-1) u0), where e^(tL) is the L2
semigroup of R2. By density and continuity, the identity holds for every u0 in H^q.
[v2 repair: (ii) uses the R2 generator assumptions as supplied with (B-L2) (IMPORTED): the L2 realization L is a
closed generator of a C_0 semigroup on the S1 part of L2, S1 Schwartz functions lie in its domain and L acts on them
pointwise, and classical L2 solutions of h' = L h are unique. Nothing else from R2 enters.]

Proof. (i) Let Phi_t be the flow of V. It is global, since V is Lipschitz. DPhi_t and its derivatives up to order 3
are bounded on bounded time intervals, because D^j DV is bounded for j <= 3. Its Jacobian determinant is e^(2ct). So
T0(t) u = exp(-integral_0^t (1 + a_W)(Phi_s X) ds) u(Phi_t X) is bounded on H^m, m = 0..3, with norm <= e^(C t). It is
therefore bounded on H^q by interpolation, and it is strongly continuous. T0 maps S1 Schwartz functions to S1 Schwartz
functions (Phi_t commutes with the S1 reflections and |Phi_t X| is comparable to |X| for bounded t). These functions
lie in the generator domain, so they form a core (Engel-Nagel, Prop. II.1.7). B is bounded on H^q, because grad
rho_bar is in C_b^infinity and U is bounded on H^m. E_W is bounded on H^q by Lemma E. Bounded perturbation gives (i)
with the same domain and the same core.
(ii) Let u_n be Schwartz with u_n -> u0 in the graph norm of L_W. For Schwartz u, W^(-1) L_W u = L(W^(-1) u) holds
pointwise. W^(-1) is bounded from H^q to L2 (W >= 1), and the L2 generator L is closed. So W^(-1) u0 is in D(L) and
L(W^(-1) u0) = W^(-1) L_W u0. Applied along u(t) = e^(t L_W) u0, which is in C^1([0, infinity); H^q), this shows that
h(t) = W^(-1) u(t) is a classical L2 solution of h' = L h. By uniqueness of classical solutions, h(t) = e^(tL) h(0). QED.

### Steps 6 and 7. Decay and conclusion

For u0 in D(L_W), E(t) := ||<D>^q e^(tL_W) u0||^2 satisfies E' <= -2(beta - epsilon) E + 2 C_eps ||W^(-1) u(t)||^2. Lemma WG
extends from the core to D(L_W) by graph-norm continuity of both sides. By Lemma S(ii) and (B-L2),
||W^(-1) u(t)|| <= e^(-beta_0 t) ||W^(-1) u0||. Put beta_1 = beta - epsilon < beta_0. Then
E(t) <= e^(-2 beta_1 t) (E(0) + C_eps ||W^(-1) u0||^2/(beta_0 - beta_1)), so

  ||<D>^q e^(t L_W) u0|| <= e^(-beta_1 t) ( ||<D>^q u0|| + (C_eps/(beta_0 - beta_1))^(1/2) ||W^(-1) u0|| ).

This extends to all u0 in H^q by density. With u0 = W F, the integral u_* = integral_0^infinity e^(t L_W)(W F) dt
converges in H^q. By Lemma S(ii), W^(-1) u_* = integral_0^infinity e^(tL) F dt = h0 (R2). This gives (O1-Hq) with
C_1 = (1/beta_1)(C_eps/(beta_0 - beta_1))^(1/2) and epsilon = beta - beta_1. The Hoelder statement follows from
H^q in C^(1,q-2) (2 < q < 3, Sobolev embedding in R^2) and the multiplier mu = <X>^sigma/W. QED (PROVED, AT-031).

## NR-3. Remarks

1. Non-explicit constant (OPEN for a quantitative version). C_eps comes from Lambda and from a finite-rank
   approximation of compact operators. A quantitative O1 needs an explicit bound
   <C_4 g, g> <= epsilon ||g||^2 + C_eps ||W^(-1)u||^2. Every compact piece has an explicit kernel or symbol: E_W
   (kernel bounds of Lemma E), the HS operator K (norm kappa = 0.728 for 007), the commutators [R_j, a_W - sigma c] and
   the (K3) remainders. So an explicit version is possible in principle. It is the first item of T2 in theory_v01
   (Lemma Q-WG).
2. Sharpness at the origin. [v2 repair: the formula holds for the normalized background of R3: S1 symmetry,
   c + d_y u_bar_2(0) = 1 and a0 = d_y rho_bar(0) > 0. Then div u_bar = 0 gives DV(0) = diag(2c - 1, 1) = diag(b, 1),
   grad rho_bar(0) = (0, a0), Kmat(0) = diag(-a0, 0) and a_W(0) = 0. For 007 these conditions are certified by AN-001.]
   N_q(0) = diag(q b - a0 - c - 1, q - c - 1), so (BG-q) forces beta <= c + 1 - q. For
   q <= a0/d this equals the exact Sobolev rate delta_q = c + 1 - q of the frozen semigroup in [S1P] P5.2 (3)-(4)
   (with lambda = b and the sources' sigma = a0/d; HISTORICAL, no status label in the source). The infinitesimal form
   is the identity <|D|^q L_fr h, |D|^q h> = (q - c - 1)|| |D|^q h ||^2 + (q d - a0)||R1 |D|^q h||^2. It is a
   restatement of the located source and is not claimed as new. For q > c + 1 the same identity shows that the frozen
   operator is not dissipative on the homogeneous space of order q (take |D|^q h with Fourier support in a thin cone
   around the xi_2-axis, where ||R1 |D|^q h|| is small). This agrees with [S1P] P5.4 (H^m unboundedness of the frozen resolvent for c + 1 = (b+3)/2 < m < b + 1;
   EXISTING_PROOFS_REFERENCE.md section 0 item 9). So the window q < c + 1 cannot be enlarged by this method.
3. Parameter window. (BG-q) at the origin needs q <= c + 1 - beta. At infinity it needs
   sigma >= q - 1 - 1/c + beta/c. Lemma E needs sigma < 1 + gamma_*. The Hoelder exponent obtained is alpha <= q - 2.
   For 007 (gamma_* = gamma_9): q in (2, c + 1 - beta] and sigma in [q - 1.5524 + beta/c, 1.4476). The FLOAT screen
   (research/bgscreen/out/bgq007.json and bgq007_table.json; polar grid r in [0.01, 3000], no enclosure) gives, at
   sigma = 1.4 and r0 in {0.25, 0.5}, a grid minimum c + 1 - q + 0.002 at the innermost grid radius r = 0.01, for
   q in [2.25, 2.7]. Since lambda_max(N_q(0)) = q - c - 1 exactly, the FLOAT reading is that the infimum is
   beta = c + 1 - q, attained at the origin. For example q = 2.5 gives beta = 0.3103 and alpha <= 1/2. At sigma = 1.0
   the binding points are far out (r about 350 to 390), below the far limit, because u_bar.X/|X|^2 and Sym D u_bar
   decay only like r^(-gamma_9). The numerical auditor cover test (numerical_audit/research/background_matrix/PROOF.md, method AT-015) is planned at
   q = 13/5, sigma = 7/5, r0^2 = 1/16, beta = 1/10. That point lies inside this window: sigma = 1.4 < 1.4476.
   [v2 update: that cover is complete (AN-022) and activates the theorem for 007 (AT-056). The r0 dependence of the
   FLOAT margin is in NR-5.]
4. What is not covered. The theorem uses no angular structure, and none of R6, R7, R10, R11. It is a k = 0
   statement. It says nothing about A_k for k != 0. AT-010 and AT-011 (normalized Hoelder-ball obstructions) do not
   apply to it, because no k enters. The solution weight sigma is at most 1 + gamma_9 here. Larger weights,
   including the prompt's sigma = 7/4, need T1-Cor-O1R9 (R9 IMPORTED, C_R not explicit).
5. Relation to AT-016. Theorem O1-Hq uses the source norm N_{q,sigma,r0}(F) with the same weight sigma as the
   solution. It does not use any R6.4 source norm, so the AT-016 scope remark (a weight below one cannot supply a
   source norm at nu > 1) does not affect it. The source and solution weights are named separately in theory_v01.
6. An origin hypothesis is necessary (new example; EXACT computation; PROVED in the scope below: AT2713, AT2714).
   Take rho_bar = 0, so u_bar = 0 and L = c X.grad - 1 (this background is not normalized: a0 = 0 and c + d_y
   u_bar_2(0) = c). Let F(r, phi) = -f(r) sin(phi) with f smooth, supported in (1, 2) and with I_f := integral_1^2
   s^(-1/c - 1) f(s) ds != 0; F is S1. Writing h = g(r) sin(phi), L h = -F becomes c r g' - g = f. Its only solution
   in L2(R^2) is g(r) = -r^(1/c) integral_r^infinity s^(-1/c - 1) f(s) ds / c, since the homogeneous solution r^(1/c)
   is not in L2 at infinity. So h0 = 0 for r >= 2 and h0 = -(I_f/c) r^(1/c) sin(phi) = -(I_f/c) r^(1/c - 1) y for
   r <= 1. This function is homogeneous of degree 1/c near the origin with a nonzero smooth angular factor, so it
   lies in H^s near 0 if and only if s < 1 + 1/c. Meanwhile -<L h, h> = (c + 1)||h||^2, so the L2 rate is c + 1. For
   this background N_q(0) = (q c - c - 1) I, so (BG-q) at the origin holds if and only if q < 1 + 1/c. The regularity
   threshold and the origin condition coincide, so no H^q statement with q >= 1 + 1/c can hold without an origin
   hypothesis, even with a good L2 rate. (The external referee referee answer an external referee answer gave the radial version; the S1
   version above is a direct recomputation.) Status (version 25). The theory auditor CONFIRMS the example independently (AT2713,
   AT2714; AT2715 OPEN; audit_v821; research/origin_example_audit of agent the theory auditor). Its proof treats L on its maximal
   L2 distributional domain, turns L into c d_t - (c + 1) by a logarithmic polar unitary map, which gives the full
   inverse bound 1/(c + 1) and the energy identity, and checks that no distribution supported at the origin is lost.
   It proves H^s membership near the origin for s < 1 + 1/c by a fractional difference integral and failure at s =
   1 + 1/c by a fixed positive contribution on disjoint dyadic annuli; smooth positive weights near the origin do not
   change this. Scope: the theory auditor reads "an origin hypothesis is necessary" as this counterexample to an estimate that
   uses only a good L2 gap and smooth forcing over a class of backgrounds containing the zero background; it does not
   classify every sufficient hypothesis, the example is not normalized, and it says nothing adverse about the
   normalized Candidate 007 or Source013 results (audit_v821). The example supplies no normalized inverse, invariant
   ball or existence result (AT2715 OPEN).

## NR-4. The Sobolev-ball framework FW-C2-Hq-v1 (pointer; the second theory agent owns the statements)

Version 1 proposed this framework as HEURISTIC. It is now developed by the second theory agent in theory_agent_2/notes/T2_O2S_v02.md (SHA256
da418c668509c239ae52e817ac021c2b251a246871efd618e67575a94418a7ff at the time of reading) and audited by the theory auditor. The ball is
K^S_{R,M} = {k S1 : ell(k) = 0, ||k|| <= R, N_{q,sigma,r0}(k) <= M}, R = ||F||_2/beta_0, T(k) = -A_k^(-1) F as in R5.
Status of the parts (the second theory agent IDs; verdicts as of this version):
- T2-GN (exact Gagliardo-Nirenberg constants, ||DU[k]||_(inf,op) <= C_GN^U ||k||^theta ||k||_(Hdot^q)^(1-theta),
  theta = (q-2)/q, closed form for C_GN): PROVED (AT-033).
- T2-P, T2-S2, T2-UT, T2-Sint: PROVED (AT-033 to AT-035).
- T2-KPW (weighted tame transport estimate; weight on the solution, finite c1 = C_q, c2 to c4, every finite
  sigma >= 0, no A_2 restriction): PROVED (AT-049, in the continuous energy-form sense of AT-051). The principal
  coefficient is q Sym DU[k] with a retained remainder C ||Da||_(C^delta) ||Wh||_(H^(q-rho)) ||Wh||_(H^q) (AT-050);
  c1 = q for the whole estimate is OPEN (HEURISTIC when used).
- T2-HS1r (restricted HS1: UT-S for S1 k with kappa(k) < beta - eps): derived by the theory auditor (AT-038) from WG + KP-W,
  reviewed by the second theory agent; CONFIRMED (AT-058): the full imported L2 inverse has weighted H^q regularity when
  kappa(k) < beta - eps, and the inverse family is uniformly bounded on N(k) <= M0 with a common positive gap; no
  numerical inverse constant is given. Unrestricted HS0: OPEN.
- T2-Sinv, T2-D1S (THR-S), T2-R5S: PROVED as implications (AT-036, AT-037).
- T2-RC, T2-RC-L2 (re-centering at a smooth correction h_*): CONFIRMED under explicit L2 center and domain
  conditions (AT-060). For a real normalized S1 Schwartz center, ||grad h_*||_inf < beta0 gives a Neumann inverse of
  norm at most 1/(beta0 - ||grad h_*||_inf); smooth bounded derivatives alone do not give an L2 center; H^q
  membership of the new source and the new background margins remain to be checked (OPEN).
Corrections that every user of the framework must carry (AT-039): the frozen perturbed matrix for A_k is
q Sym D(V + U[k]) + Kmat - (c + 1 + a_W + U[k].grad log W) I; the full stationary derivative adds K(k) (Kmat with
grad rho_bar replaced by grad(rho_bar + k)); the absorption term is c1 C_GN^U R^theta (c_W M)^(1-theta) with no extra q.
Withdrawn from version 1: "the framework is expected to need sigma < 1". KP-W puts the weight on the solution and holds
for every finite sigma (AT-049); the remaining weight ceiling is sigma < 1 + gamma_* from WG (Lemma E).
The weak fixed-point step is IMPORTED from R5 (the coordinator 19:13Z); no Sobolev analog is proved here.

## NR-5. The absorption obstruction at Candidate 007 and the weaker-norm question (HEURISTIC; numbers FLOAT)

Corrected in version 3 per the theory auditor AT-077, AT-078 (research/weaker_norm_review/PROOF.md in the theory auditor) and AT-080. The
version 2 text is frozen at theory_agent_1/history/THEORY_NEW_RESULTS_v2.md.
Computations: theory_agent_1/research/bgscreen/ (bgq_r0scan.py, nf_fine.py, nf_fine_post.py, closure_table.py; outputs in
out/). Grid values only, no enclosure. R = 0.18814198 is the AN-010 upper bound for ||F||_2/beta_0 at 007.

5.1 GN constant as q decreases to 2 (EXACT asymptotics of the AT-033 closed form). With theta = (q-2)/q,
  C_GN^U(q) = (q/(q-2)) (pi/2)^(theta/2) (sqrt(pi(q-2))/2)^(1-theta) / (2 pi sqrt 2) = (q-2)^(-1/2)/(2 sqrt(2 pi)) (1 + o(1)).
  Values: 1.4574 (q = 2.02), 0.9564 (2.05), 0.7113 (2.1), 0.6060 (2.15), 0.5448 (2.2), 0.5038 (2.25), 0.4740 (2.3),
  0.4328 (2.4), 0.4051 (2.5), 0.3847 (2.6). The blow-up of this strain-level constant reflects the failure of H^2 in
  W^(1,infinity) in two dimensions. Only the derivative and strain constants diverge like (q-2)^(-1/2); the value GN
  constants (C_inf in the AT-064 family, and the AT-033 value constants) stay finite as q decreases to 2 (AT-080).
5.2 The necessary test (the second theory agent T2_O2S_v02 section 8 (d); c1 >= q reviewed at AT-072; the test is CONFIRMED at AT-061).
  Any M satisfying (D1-S) and (D2-S) obeys N(h0) R^((q-2)/2) < [beta/(c1 C_GN^U)]^(q/2). Write allowed(q, beta) :=
  [beta/(q C_GN^U)]^(q/2) R^(-(q-2)/2). At fixed R and beta, as q -> 2, allowed -> 0 like 2.51 beta (q-2)^(1/2), and
  the factor R^((q-2)/2) -> 1 (the smallness of R is lost). This fixed-R asymptotic is CONFIRMED (AT-078). A best q
  over the changing source, weight, gap and weak constants is not established by it (AT-078); the statement "the best
  q is interior" is HEURISTIC.
5.3 FLOAT data at 007. N2(sigma, r0) := ||<D>^2 (W F)||_2 is a lower bound for N_{q,sigma,r0}(F) at every q >= 2
  (EXACT same-weight inequality, since <xi>^q >= <xi>^2, CONFIRMED at AT-078; the values are FLOAT). It is about 83.5
  at sigma = 0 and grows with the weight (133.7 at (0.9, 1), 211.4 at (1.4, 1), 470.9 at (1.0, 1/4), 1256.7 at
  (1.4, 1/4)). Half of ||D^2 F||_2^2 lies in r < 1.02 and 99% in r < 3.8. A weight-free comparison with the
  unweighted Hessian is not automatic: AT-057 proves ||D^2 F||_2 <= C_inv N_q(F), C_inv = (1 + 2 C1^2 + C2^2)^(1/2),
  C1 = sigma/(2 r0), C2 = (sqrt 2 sigma + sigma^2/4)/r0^2, and refutes the unit comparison in general. The FLOAT
  statement is only that every sampled N2(sigma, r0) is at least 83.5, the sigma = 0 value.
  The ratio gap := (N2/beta)/allowed(q, beta) below uses N2/beta as a source-based proxy for N(h0). It is not a lower
  bound for N(h0) (AT-078). The certified exclusions of the coarse scalar criterion are source-based and proxy-free:
  AT-075, AT-080 and AT-088 (theory_v01 T3.4 and NR-6.3 below). The proxy gap at the best sampled (sigma, r0) for each q:
  | q | best (sigma, r0) | beta (FLOAT) | binding r | N2 | allowed | gap |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 2.02 | (1.2, 1) | 0.695 | 0.73 | 165.8 | 0.236 | 1010 |
  | 2.05 | (1.2, 1) | 0.656 | 0.73 | 165.8 | 0.340 | 744 |
  | 2.1 | (1.4, 1) | 0.668 | 0.73 | 211.4 | 0.467 | 678 |

  | 2.2 | (1.4, 1) | 0.541 | 0.73 | 211.4 | 0.492 | 794 |
  | 2.3 | (1.2, 1/2) | 0.511 | origin | 327.1 | 0.537 | 1193 |

  The gap uses the lower bound N2 for N_q(F), so at q > 2 the true proxy gap is larger (the second numerics agent's interpolation uppers
  N_q(F) <= 1735 at P-Q2 and 7375 at P-Q1 give 2.9e4 and about 1.6e5). The minimum, about 7e2, is near q = 2.1 to 2.15.
  Every "origin" row satisfies the necessary condition (5.4) sigma >= q(1 - 1/c).
5.4 The r0 window (corrected). Lemma E puts no condition on r0 (only sigma < 1 + gamma_*). At the origin a_W(0) = 0, so
  the origin value lambda_max(N_q(0)) = q - c - 1 does not depend on (sigma, r0), and the global margin is at most
  c + 1 - q (AT-070). The limit of the frozen gap at infinity is 1 + c(1 + sigma - q) (AT-070). Hence a globally
  binding origin needs
      sigma >= q (1 - 1/c)                                                (EXACT necessary condition; AT-070, AT-078)
  with values 0.93998 (q = 21/10), 1.00713 (9/4), 1.07427 (12/5), 1.11903 (5/2), 1.16379 (13/5) (FLOAT decimals of the
  exact expression). The condition is necessary and not sufficient: it does not control the intermediate region.
  The version 2 sentence "for r0 <= 1/2 and sigma >= 1.1 the origin binds (beta = c + 1 - q)" is REFUTED as stated:
  at q = 5/2 and sigma = 11/10 the origin cannot bind for any r0 > 0, because (7/5) c > 5/2 (AT-078). The FLOAT screen
  data are unchanged and give no exhaustive radius window: beta falls by about 0.32 per doubling of r0 beyond r0 = 1
  at the sampled (q, sigma); beta < 0 at r0 = 4 for every sampled q in [2.2, 2.5] and sigma in [0.9, 1.4]; beta > 0 at
  r0 = 2 only for q <= 2.3 (and sigma >= 1.2 at q = 2.3). The sampled N2 at sigma = 1.0 decreases in r0 (470.9 at
  r0 = 1/4, 142.8 at r0 = 1, 101.6 at r0 = 2), but no monotonicity in r0 is claimed. AT-077 REFUTES general monotonic
  decrease of a weighted H^q source norm in r0 (for every q > 1 and sigma > 0 there is a normalized S1 Schwartz source
  and two radii where the larger radius gives the strictly larger norm). The 007 trend is OPEN beyond the samples and
  individual certified bounds.
5.5 Can the strong part be taken in a weaker norm? (HEURISTIC analysis, for the coordinator 19:56Z item 4(c).)
  (a) What the weak fixed point needs. R5 needs a convex set that is weakly compact in L2, invariant under T, on which T
  is weakly continuous. Weak continuity of k -> U[k].grad T(k) needs some local compactness of T(k) uniformly on the
  set; an H^s bound with any s > 0 suffices for that step alone (CONFIRMED for that step at AT-078; identifying the
  limit with the full inverse, normalization and closedness of the set remain separate).
  (b) The supplied route needs Lipschitz-type control of U[k]. A k-uniform bound of a positive-order norm of
  T(k) = -A_k^(-1) F by an energy estimate for A_k = L + U[k].grad meets the commutator [Lambda^s, U[k].grad], which is
  controlled by ||DU[k]||_inf (KP-W at order q; the same at orders 0 < s < 1). With only ||k||_2 <= R the velocity U[k]
  has no bounded gradient. This is a requirement of the supplied Sobolev commutator route. It is not a proof that every
  positive-order inverse estimate must come from that route, and the absence of isotropic smoothing does not determine
  the regularity of this particular inverse image (AT-078). OPEN; no alternative route is proposed.
  (c) Weakest Sobolev control of ||DU[k]||_inf in two dimensions: H^q with q > 2 through GN (constant as in 5.1), or the
  endpoint Besov space B^2_(2,1). The Besov endpoint removes the (q-2)^(-1/2) blow-up but also the factor R^theta, so
  the condition becomes c1 C_B M < beta with M >= N_B(h0). The conclusion "no gain at 007" is HEURISTIC: the embedding
  constant and the dyadic convention are not fixed, and a source norm comparison does not lower-bound the inverse
  image (AT-078). No Besov source threshold is supplied.
  (d) A frequency or radial split F = F_lo + F_hi with F_hi kept in L2 only does not help through the supplied route:
  -A_k^(-1) F_hi is then controlled only in L2, and the ball must contain k with uncontrolled DU[k]. A split helps the
  supplied route only if the high part is absorbed into the background, which is re-centering (5.6). Blanket
  exclusion of splitting is OPEN (AT-078).
  (e) Solution-level matrix test. For k = h0 the principal perturbation of the frozen matrix is
  q Sym DU[h0] - (U[h0].grad log W) I (AT-039, AT-050). If a rigorously enclosed matrix at the exact h0 fails to have a
  positive margin at a specified tuple, then a uniform positive-matrix criterion on any set containing h0 fails at that
  tuple (AT-078). Limitations: a FLOAT failure at h_N needs a certified bound on h_N - h0 first; failure at one q does
  not imply failure at every q; and the full inverse may exist by estimates outside this sufficient criterion.
  The version 2 "necessary scalar level" ||Sym DU[h0]||_(inf,op) < beta/q is REFUTED as a necessary consequence of
  principal matrix positivity (AT-078): at the certified P-BG2 there is a smooth, compactly supported, normalized S1 k
  with ell(k) = 0, supported away from the origin in the far-field matrix slack, such that every t k (0 <= t <= 1) keeps
  the global frozen matrix gap 1/4 while ||Sym DU[k]||_(inf,op) >= 1/8 > beta/q = 1/10. The pointwise matrix condition
  is the operative one; the scalar is only a sufficient simplification.
5.6 Re-centering (the coordinator 19:26Z item 4(d); the second theory agent T2-RC). The identity is CONFIRMED at AT-060 under its hypotheses:
  an eligible L2 center h_* with ell(h_*) = 0 and the needed L2 and decay assumptions, the Neumann factorization when
  ||grad h_*||_inf < beta_0, the complete background hypotheses for rho_* = rho_bar + h_*, normalization and source
  membership ("smooth S1 correction" alone omits the L2 and decay assumptions, AT-078). Then h = h_* + d is a fixed
  point of T if and only if d is a fixed point of the same map for the background rho_* and the residual
  F_* = F + L h_* + U[h_*].grad h_*. The separately controlled small quantity is R_* = ||F_*||_2/beta_0*, with
  beta_0* >= beta_0 - ||grad h_*||_inf (T2-RC-L2). N(F_*) < infinity is only qualitative; the self-map needs the
  quantitative inequalities. The large part DU[h_*] moves into (BG-q)_* for rho_*, which is tested pointwise without
  interpolation loss. By 5.3 the needed reduction is in the strong residual: at q = 5/2 the proxy test asks
  N(F_*) <= beta_* [beta_*/(q C_GN^U)]^(5/4) R_*^(-1/4), that is N(F_*) <= about 0.22 for beta_* = 0.31 and
  R_* = 1e-2 (HEURISTIC, FLOAT). Lowering ||F||_2 alone moves the threshold only through R^(-(q-2)/2), a factor
  10^((q-2)/2) per decade of R (2.0 at q = 2.6, 1.12 at q = 2.1). NR-6.3 gives the q-uniform form of this statement.
5.7 Re-centering at h0 itself (the second theory agent T2_O2S_v02 section 9 (v); wording per AT-078 and AT-072). Here F_* = U[h0].grad h0.
  For a known h0 with W h0 in H^q the justified conclusion is W(U[h0].grad h0) in H^(q-1). The input W grad h0 in H^q
  is a sufficient input for the H^q source class; it is not supplied by the current theorem (O1 at order q + 1 would
  need BG-(q+1), whose origin cap requires q + 1 < c + 1, and WG needs q < 3). Failure of the current BG-(q+1) origin
  criterion does not prove that h0 lacks this regularity.

Setting as in NR-0 and theory_v01 T2.1: L h = V.grad h - h + grad rho_bar.U[h], A_k = L + U[k].grad, DV(0) = diag(b, 1),
DU[h](0) = diag(-ell(h), ell(h)), d_y(L h)(0) = a0 ell(h) (R3, IMPORTED). For the actual h0 the forward Duhamel identity
h0(X) = int_0^infinity e^(-s) g(Phi_s X) ds, g = F + grad rho_bar.U[h0], holds with vanishing boundary term (AT-080,
CONFIRMED).

6.1 Zero vertical rate (EXACT scaling for the frozen transport T0 = b x d_x + y d_y; CLAIMED as a remark). On a
  seminorm of order s in L^p the Duhamel weight has vertical rate s - 1 - 2c/p and horizontal rate (before Riesz
  damping) b s - 1 - 2c/p. At the Lipschitz level (s = 1, p = infinity) the vertical rate is exactly 0; it is positive in
  every C^(1,alpha), which is the scaling behind the IMPORTED R6 negative result. At p = 2 the vertical rate is negative
  iff s < c + 1, which is the BG-q origin cap beta <= c + 1 - q (AT-070). So no pointwise matrix inequality at the
  Lipschitz level has a positive margin on all of R^2: the vertical entry of Sym DV(0) - I is 0. AT-080 confirms this
  observation and notes that it alone proves no impossibility for the full operator.
6.2 Model Hoelder loss (EXACT for the model (T0 - 1) h = -g; CLAIMED, new). The bounded solution is
  h(X) = int_0^infinity e^(-s) g(e^(bs) x, e^s y) ds, and on the y axis (y > 0)
      h(0, y) = y int_y^infinity g(0, u) u^(-2) du,     h_y(0) = int_0^infinity (g(0, u) - g(0, 0)) u^(-2) du.
  (a) If g_y(0) = 0 and 0 < delta < 1: |h_y(0)| <= [d_y g]_(C^delta)/(delta (1 + delta)) + 2 ||g||_inf. The order
      1/delta is attained (g(0, u) = u^(1+delta)/(1+delta) near 0).
  (b) No bound without a Hoelder loss: g(0, u) = u/log(1/u) near 0 (C^1, g_y(0) = 0, compact support, either parity in
      y, even in x) gives h_y(0) = +infinity, while h is bounded with compact support, hence in L^2. So no estimate
      ||grad h||_inf <= C (||g||_(C^1) + ||h||_2) holds for the model.
  (c) For the full operator the nonlocal term does not obviously remove the loss: d_y(grad rho_bar.U[h])(0) =
      a0 ell(h) vanishes on the normalized class, the angular damping a0 R_1^2 is zero on vertical frequencies, and
      ell(z) = 0 does not force z_y(0) = 0 (AT-080 gives an S1 example with z_y(0) = 1, ell(z) = 0, U[z] = 0 near 0).
      HEURISTIC for the full operator. Read as a margin, beta_L <~ delta (1 + delta) for the vertical gradient at the
      origin.
6.3 GN low tier and the q-uniform ceiling (EXACT given the cap and the cited formulas; CLAIMED, new).
  (a) Any H^q tier with a positive BG-q margin has q < c + 1 (AT-070). So the R-exponent (q - 2)/2 of NT2-F is below
      p_max = (c - 1)/2 (EXACT rational, 0.40515960509999904...), and with R >= R_floor = 0.3766893/(c + 1)
      (AT-080) the L^2 smallness can contribute at most R_floor^(-p_max) <= 2.257419 (UB).
  (b) A two-tier set whose low tier (weighted C^(1,alpha)) is controlled by GN interpolation from the high tier,
      H_L(z) <= C R^(theta') M_H^(1-theta') with theta' = (q - 2 - alpha)/q, needs
      N(F) R^(theta'/(1-theta')) <= beta' (eps_L/C)^(1/(1-theta')), and theta'/(1-theta') = (q - 2 - alpha)/(2 + alpha)
      <= (q - 2)/2. This is NT2-F with other constants and an R-exponent no larger. A two-tier set beats NT2-F only if
      its low tier has its own estimate (the strong LK of 6.5).
  (c) q-uniform source ceiling for the coarse criterion of FW-C2-HqK-v1. With T(beta) = beta [beta/(q C*_sym(q))]^(q/2),
      C*_sym = B1/4, B1 = [1/(4 q sin(pi s))]^(1/2) t^(-t/2) s^(-s/2), s = 2/q, t = 1 - s (AT-064 family as in AT-080),
      the criterion at any (q, sigma, r0) with 2 < q < c + 1 can hold only if
          N_(q,sigma,r0)(F) < N_*(q) := T(c + 1 - q) R_floor^(-(q-2)/2),
      since T increases in beta, beta <= c + 1 - q and R^p >= R_floor^p (AT-080 eq. (2)). Interval evaluation over 4000
      q-intervals on [2 + 1e-4, c + 1 - 1e-4] with analytic end bounds: sup_(2 < q < c+1) N_*(q) <= 1.177171 (UB),
      attained near q = 2.1705 (FLOAT location). N_*(21/10) <= 1.097831, N_*(12/5) <= 0.7602914, N_*(5/2) <= 0.4729857,
      N_*(13/5) <= 0.2189166 (UB). For a strain coefficient kappa q C*_sym with kappa >= 1/sqrt(2) (AT-081), the
      ceiling is <= 1.915742 (UB). The ceiling does not depend on c_W, C_eps_bar, c_4', eps or (sigma, r0), except
      through N(F) and beta <= c + 1 - q.
  (d) New certified failure rows (the coordinator 21:08Z item 2; formulas (3) of AT-080, AN-033 source lower bounds; L is an
      exact terminating rational strictly below the AN-033 bound; ratio LB, T and kappa_cap UB, c1_cap UB in the AT-033
      form of C_GN^U as in AT-080's table):
      | (q, sigma, r0) | L | L R_floor^p, LB | T(c+1-q), UB | ratio, LB | kappa_cap, UB | c1_cap, UB |
      |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
      | P-S1 (21/10, 1, 1/2) | 9.699083227771 | 8.771867 | 0.9928794 | 8.834776 | 0.125563 | 0.09114893 |
      | (5/2, 7/5, 1/2) | 59.503510767543 | 36.00389 | 0.2861903 | 125.804 | 0.02090472 | 0.01708697 |
      Control rows reproduce AT-080: (12/5, 1, 2) ratio 9.305831, kappa_cap 0.155849, c1_cap 0.1235362; (13/5, 7/5, 1/4)
      805.7571, 0.005813777, 0.004900969. P-S1 has the smallest certified failure ratio among the ten certified tuples.
      AT-088 independently CONFIRMS the P-S1 exclusion by a coarser exact argument. With N(F) unchanged, the R at which
      L R^p would equal T is <= 1.597041e-20 at P-S1 and <= 5.351176e-10 at (5/2, 7/5, 1/2) (UB).
  Reading (HEURISTIC). The decisive quantity for the coarse criterion is the strong source norm: a reduction by a
  factor of at least 8.83 at the certified tuples, and about 70 against the q-uniform ceiling on FLOAT N_2 >= 83.5, is

6.4 Background conditions for a strong LK (the answer to "which pointwise condition"). No BG-q-type pointwise matrix
  condition exists (6.1). The route needs the list BG-L0 (R3 normalization), BG-L1 (R6.3, R6.4: for 007 holds for

## NR-7. T1-Thm-HFNEC: the fixed-norm rate ceiling is sharp (PROVED, AT-211)

Status (version 5): AT-300 CONFIRMED the integration of this statement with the retained full inverse and common-center
hypotheses and an explicit R > 0.

Source: theory_agent_1/research/qwg/QWG_v02.md section 2 (the snapshot 5ba2d4fd6caa... audited in AT-211; AT-217 REFUTED one
intermediate sentence, see Remark 3). The theory auditor's standalone proof of the abstract form: the theory auditor
research/sharp_matrix_rate_ceiling/PROOF.md.

Theorem (abstract form, as CONFIRMED in AT-211). Let q > 0, r0 > 0, sigma >= 0, W(X) = (1 + |X|^2/r0^2)^(sigma/2),
J = (1 - Delta)^(1/2), B_j = d_j J^(-1) and R_j = d_j |D|^(-1). Let A and K be bounded continuous real symmetric 2 x 2
matrix fields and d a bounded continuous real function on R^2 with A(PX) = P A(X) P, K(PX) = P K(X) P and
d(PX) = d(X) for P = diag(-1, 1) and P = diag(1, -1). Let E be a real quadratic form on the real S1 Schwartz functions
(even in x, odd in y) with
    E(g) = q int (Bg)^T A (Bg) + int (Rg)^T K (Rg) - int d g^2 + Re <C g, g>,   C compact on L^2.
Put H = q A + K - d I and beta = -sup_X lambda_max H(X). If for a real b and a finite C0 >= 0
    E(g) <= -b ||g||^2 + C0 ||W^(-1) J^(-q) g||^2   for every real S1 Schwartz g,
then b <= beta.

Application. For the background of T1-Lemma-WG (NR-2) under its hypotheses other than (BG-q), namely (BG-reg;
gamma_*), the R1 hypotheses, 2 < q < 3, 0 <= sigma < 1 + gamma_* and the S1 symmetry, the AT-029 decomposition has
A = Sym DV, K = Kmat, d = c + 1 + a_W, so H = N_q, and with g = <D>^q u the hypothesis is the k = 0 weak inequality
of C^opt(b) (theory_v04 T2.2). Hence C^opt(b) = +infinity for every b > beta. With T1-Lemma-WG, the set
{b : C^opt(b) < infinity} contains (-infinity, beta) and is contained in (-infinity, beta]. The endpoint b = beta is
not decided. The statement concerns the fixed norm N_(q,sigma,r0) and the weak term ||W^(-1) u||; for an equivalent
energy norm it is OPEN.

Proof (outline; full text in QWG_v02 section 2). Four reflected packets
g_n = S1-symmetrization of n^(1/2) phi(n^(1/2)(X - X*)) cos(n omega.X), normalized, at an off-axis X*: the weak term
is O(n^(-2q)) relative to ||g_n||^2, the compact part tends to 0 by weak convergence, and the principal part tends to
omega^T H(X*) omega, the same for all four images by the covariance of A, K and d. Taking the supremum over omega and
X* gives sup lambda_max H <= -b.

Corollaries (each from NR-7 and the CONFIRMED input named).
(a) For every normalized background in the class of AT-167, beta <= c + 1 - q - max(q(2c - 2) - a_0, 0)
    <= c + 1 - q, which is 0.710319210199998... at q = 21/10 for c = c_l (EXACT form; AT-087, AT-167, AT-266). So
    C^opt(b) = +infinity for b > c + 1 - q.
(b) Unchanged 007 at P-S2 = (21/10, 7/5, 1): 3/5 <= beta < 0.6588409179 (AN-056, AT-172; AN-068, AT-199), so
    C^opt(b) = +infinity for b >= 0.6588409179, and equally for Cbar^opt on any coefficient ball that contains k = 0.
(c) The tame term of AT-114 (1) vanishes at k = 0, so every admissible rate b of AT-114 (1) satisfies b <= beta of the
    center itself.

Remarks. 1. A universal rate loss of every bound of C^opt does not follow from the construction constraint b + L <= beta
of QWG_v02 (AT-215 REFUTED that inference). 2. b = 0.7103 lies 1.92102e-5 below the cap and is an interior rate
(AT-215, AT-266). 3. N_q is covariant under the S1 reflections, but grad rho_bar is not a vector under the y reflection;
the QWG_v02 sentence that called it one is REFUTED (AT-217). The theorem is unaffected.

## NR-8. T1-Lem-LF1FZ: frozen-symbol monotonicity and two pointwise form inequalities (PROVED, AT-212)

Status (version 5): AT-300 CONFIRMED the integration of this statement with the retained full inverse and common-center
hypotheses and an explicit R > 0.

Source: QWG_v02 section 3. Notation: S = Sym D u_bar (traceless), s = |S| (operator norm), theta as in NR-0,
t = |xi|^2/<xi>^2 in [0, 1), B_j = d_j J^(-1) (written Rt in NR-0).
- LF-1. The frozen symbol n(X, omega, t) = q t (c + omega^T S(X) omega) + omega^T Kmat(X) omega - (c + 1 + a_W(X)),
  |omega| = 1, is nondecreasing in t at every X with s(X) <= c. (The t-coefficient is
  q (c + omega^T S omega) >= q (c - s).)
- FZ. For real g in L^2 and measurable w with 0 <= w <= 1:
  (i) int w (Rg)^T Kmat (Rg) <= int w theta |Rg|^2 <= (sup theta) ||g||^2;
  (ii) q int w (Bg)^T Sym DV (Bg) <= q int w (c + s) |Bg|^2.
- Scope (AT-212). For 007 the premise sup s < c is sampled (sup s = 1.1455, FLOAT grid maximum; not certified), and
  a frozen-symbol bound is not an operator estimate. The far-zone bound built on FZ (AT-213) needs a globally
  Lipschitz cutoff and coefficient extrema on the whole support of w, including the transition annulus; the version
  without the complementary theta term is REFUTED (AT-213).

## NR-9. T1-Rule-dN: a necessary condition for criterion (6_l) form 1 (PROVED for d_hf > 0, AT-261)

Status (version 5): AT-300 CONFIRMED the integration of this statement with the retained full inverse and common-center
hypotheses and an explicit R > 0.

Setting: the second theory agent's criterion (6_l) form 1 asks for M > 0 with
    N + (b(M) Cbar)^(1/2) R + T1 C_grad d M^(2 - theta) <= b(M) M,   b(M) = b0 - R D_low - d_hf M,
with N, Cbar, R, D_low, T1 C_grad d >= 0, 0 < theta < 1 and d_hf > 0 (b(M) >= 0 is part of the criterion, since its
square root appears).
Statement. If such an M exists, then d_hf N <= b0^2/4. With a = b0 - R D_low, also
    d_hf [N + (b(M) Cbar)^(1/2) R + T1 C_grad d M^(2 - theta)] <= a^2/4   at that M.
Proof. Every term on the left of (6_l) is nonnegative, so N <= b(M) M = (a - d_hf M) M <= (b0 - d_hf M) M. For
d_hf > 0 the quadratic (x - d_hf M) M in M is at most x^2/(4 d_hf) for every real x. Multiply by d_hf. QED.
Remarks (AT-261). The right side may not be replaced by b(M)^2/4 (exact counterexample in AT-261). At N = 0.0023 and
b0 = 0.6588409179 the bound is d_hf <= 47.181669032534... (47.19 is a safe decimal upper; 47.18 is not).

## NR-10. T1-Rule-HFQC0: what the HF-Q direct test can absorb (PROVED with the displayed constants, AT-262)

Status (version 5): AT-300 CONFIRMED the integration of this statement with the retained full inverse and common-center
hypotheses and an explicit R > 0.

Setting (AT-229, AT-234, AT-237; the second theory agent T2_CEPS_v02): q = 21/10, sigma = 7/5, W = (1 + |X|^2)^(7/10),
N_F = ||J^q(WF)||_2, the L^2 radius R = ||F||_2/beta_0 (beta_0 the L^2 rate of the inverse, so ||h||_2 <= R),
and the HF-Q direct test: there is M > 0 with Phi(M) = [b0 - L(R, M)] M > 0 and
    C0 <= M (Phi(M) - N_F)/R^2,
where b0 > 0 and C0 >= 0 are the rate and weak constant of the base energy (every C0 >= C^opt(b0) may be used) and L
is the HF-Q loss. Loss floor: L(R, M) > lambda M for every R > 0 and M > 0, with lambda = lambda_0 = a(1/2) =
0.868992902989268... for the refined loss (AT-234) and every pair with Bc > 1/2, where
a(Bc) = (7441/5000) (c_B S1 + sqrt(2) Bc S0 sigma/2), c_B = 53/100, S0 = 1/(2 sqrt(pi (q - 1))), S1 = S0/sqrt(q - 2)
(AT-238), and lambda = a_min = 1.14885 for the AT-229 loss and its ten certified pairs.
Statement. If the test passes, then N_F < b0^2/(4 lambda), and with m_+ = (b0 + sqrt(b0^2 - 3 lambda N_F))/(3 lambda),
    C0 ||F||_2^2 = C0 beta_0^2 R^2 < beta_0^2 m_+^2 (2 lambda m_+ - b0) <= 4 b0^3 beta_0^2/(27 lambda^2).
Proof. For M > 0, Phi(M) < (b0 - lambda M) M <= b0^2/(4 lambda), and the test needs Phi(M) >= N_F, so
N_F < b0^2/(4 lambda). Next C0 R^2 <= M (Phi(M) - N_F) < f(M) := b0 M^2 - lambda M^3 - N_F M. On M > 0, f' = 0 at
m_+- = (b0 +- sqrt(b0^2 - 3 lambda N_F))/(3 lambda) (real, since N_F < b0^2/(4 lambda)); f(0+) = 0, f decreases up to
m_- and has its maximum on M > 0 at m_+, and using N_F = 2 b0 m_+ - 3 lambda m_+^2,
f(m_+) = m_+^2 (2 lambda m_+ - b0). At N_F = 0, m_+ = 2 b0/(3 lambda) and f(m_+) = 4 b0^3/(27 lambda^2);
f(m_+) decreases in N_F and tends to 0 as N_F -> b0^2/(4 lambda). QED.
Values (exact expressions in the FLOAT input beta_0 = 2.09, decimals rounded up): with lambda_0 at b0 = 3/5 the bound is
0.18511 at N_F = 0, and below 0.14294, 0.12649 and 0.07159 at N_F = 0.0214, 0.03 and 0.06; 0.24508 at the limiting
b0 = 0.6588409179; 0.106 for the AT-229 loss at b0 = 3/5. The bound limits what this test can absorb; it is not a
necessary condition for closure.

## NR-11. T1-Rule-HFQcap: center-independent ceiling of the HF-Q source norm at P-S2 (PROVED: AT-260, AT-271, AT-274)

Status (version 5): AT-300 CONFIRMED the integration of this statement with the retained full inverse and common-center
hypotheses and an explicit R > 0.

Hypotheses: q = 21/10, sigma = 7/5, r0 = 1 and c = c_l; a normalized background that satisfies the retained
hypotheses of HF-NEC (AT-211, NR-7) and of the AT-229 (4) closure framework (the audited full inverse, domain,
weight and frozen Hoelder hypotheses, AT-237), and for which the HF-Q direct test of NR-10 passes with rate b0 > 0
and some C0 >= 0, R > 0, M > 0.
Statement (original test, c_W M input). N_F < (c + 1 - q)^2/(4 lambda_0) = 0.145154632058422... < 0.14516 for the
refined loss with any pair Bc > 1/2, and N_F < (c + 1 - q)^2/(4 a_min) < 0.10980 for the AT-229 loss and its ten pairs.
Statement (CE test). If H5 is replaced by H5' of the second theory agent's Corollary HF-Q-CE (b0 - L_B(R, Kbar(R, M)) > 0, AT-273),
then N_F < (c + 1 - q)^2/(4 lambda_1) = 0.2160191234293435851... < 0.21602, lambda_1 = lambda_0/c_W =
0.583922122691... (Proposition CEIL, AT-274).
Proof. The base energy with rate b0 and finite C0 is the hypothesis of NR-7, so b0 <= beta <= c + 1 - q (NR-7 (a)).
By NR-10, N_F < b0^2/(4 lambda) <= (c + 1 - q)^2/(4 lambda). For the CE test, L_B(R, K) > lambda_1 K (AT-274) and every
uniform Kbar is at least M (AT-274), so the CE loss exceeds lambda_1 M and NR-10 applies with lambda_1. QED.
