Scope. The bound holds for every center in the class, every R and every C^opt(b0); it limits route Q only and is not
a nonexistence statement. AT-271 REFUTED the inference that a target well below 0.15 is necessary once the CE test is
used. Uses: route Q is excluded at P-S2 for unchanged 007 (N_F > 261.62, AT-188), for C5 + b7 (first
N_F >= 7.1192735597, AN-083, AT-258; AT-260 for the original test, AT-274 for the CE test; now N_F > 14.736340388,
AT-324) and for 007 + b7 (N_F > 9.629508734, AT-324; exclusion AT-287).

## NR-12. Power-tail lower bound for the weighted source norm (the e-sector rule; PROVED in the corrected form, AT-299)

Statement (as CONFIRMED in AT-299; the theory auditor research/nr12_finite_tail_bound/PROOF.md). Let q >= 0, r0 > 0, sigma >= 0,
W = (1 + |X|^2/r0^2)^(sigma/2), J = (1 - Delta)^(1/2) and N = ||J^q (W F)||_2 < infinity on R^2. Let rho > 0, e real,
g in L^2(S^1), and suppose F(r, theta) = r^e g(theta) + G(r, theta) for r >= rho. For finite T > rho put
kappa = 2 e + 2 sigma + 2 and I(rho, T) = (T^kappa - rho^kappa)/kappa if kappa != 0, I(rho, T) = log(T/rho) if
kappa = 0. Then
    N >= ||W F 1_[rho,T]||_2 >= max(0, r0^(-sigma) ||g||_(L^2(S^1)) I(rho, T)^(1/2) - ||W G 1_[rho,T]||_2),
and all quantities on the right are finite.
(a) If kappa < 0, then W G 1_(r >= rho) is in L^2 and the bound holds with T = infinity and
    I(rho, infinity) = rho^kappa/(-kappa).
(b) If kappa >= 0 and g is nonzero, a finite N forces ||W G 1_(r >= rho)||_2 = infinity. Equivalently, a finite
    weighted remainder together with a nonzero model with kappa >= 0 makes N infinite. In particular a nonzero exact
    exterior power with e >= -sigma - 1 has infinite N.
Proof. ||W F||_2 <= N by Plancherel and (1 + |xi|^2)^q >= 1. On [rho, T] the model W r^e g is in L^2, so W G is too,
and the reverse triangle inequality with W(r) >= (r/r0)^sigma and polar integration gives the display. For (a),
W(r)^2 <= r0^(-2 sigma) r^(2 sigma) (1 + r0^2/rho^2)^sigma on r >= rho makes the model weighted L^2 on the exterior;
let T -> infinity. For (b), I(rho, T) -> infinity. QED (AT-299).
Version 4 displayed the exterior subtraction without a finite-remainder hypothesis. AT-299 REFUTED that display: with
q = sigma = 0, r0 = rho = 1, F = 0, e = 0, g = sin(theta) and G = -g, both subtracted terms are infinite.
Shell ratios (AT-295). For the homogeneous weight r0^(-sigma) r^sigma the energy of r^e g beyond rho' > rho is the
fraction (rho'/rho)^kappa of that beyond rho. For W with sigma > 0 the actual-weight ratio is strictly smaller at
finite radius and tends to the homogeneous one as rho -> infinity. For c = c_l the (3,1) exponent is
e = 4/c - 5 = -2.790444924..., so kappa = -0.780889848... at P-S2 (homogeneous half-energy radius 2^(1/0.780889...) rho
= 2.43 rho; dyadic factor 0.582) and kappa = -1.580889848... at P-S1.
Scope (AT-295, AT-299). A nonzero coefficient alone gives no positive norm floor: a smooth cutoff power times a small
constant has a nonzero surviving tail and arbitrarily small norm when kappa < 0. A finite mesh alone cannot control an
unmeasured exterior, whether or not one selected sector vanishes; analytic remainder estimates can control a nonzero
integrable sector. The certified lowers of the registered b7 centers come from complete annuli (AN-083, AN-089,
AN-090, AT-258, AT-286, AT-323, AT-324), not from this rule.

## NR-13. The C0 bound for the HF-Q-CE test (PROVED as a necessary condition of the conditional test; AT-294, AT-300)

Status (version 5): AT-300 CONFIRMED the integration of this statement with the retained full inverse and common-center
hypotheses and an explicit R > 0.

Hypotheses: those of NR-10 with the test of the second theory agent's Corollary HF-Q-CE (CONFIRMED as a conditional implication,
AT-273): Phi_CE(M) = [b0 - L_B(R, Kbar(R, M))] M > 0 and C0 <= M (Phi_CE(M) - N_F)/R^2, where Kbar(R, M) is a
strict root witness of Corollary CE-B.
Statement. If the test passes, then N_F < b0^2/(4 lambda_1) and
    C0 ||F||_2^2 < beta_0^2 m_+^2 (2 lambda_1 m_+ - b0) <= 4 b0^3 beta_0^2/(27 lambda_1^2),
with m_+ as in NR-10 for lambda = lambda_1 = 0.583922122691...
Proof. L_B(R, K) > lambda_1 K (Proposition CEIL, AT-274) and Kbar(R, M) >= M (AT-274: no uniform bound below M on
the normalized ball), so L_B(R, Kbar(R, M)) > lambda_1 M, and the proof of NR-10 applies with lambda_1. QED.
Values (beta_0 = 209/100 as a stipulated input, decimals rounded up): at b0 = 3/5 the bound is 0.40996 at N_F = 0, and
below 0.34677, 0.32187 and 0.23733 at N_F = 0.0214, 0.03 and 0.06. AT-294 encloses the exact envelopes as about
0.4099517000, 0.3467661540, 0.3218644970 and 0.2373288538 and CONFIRMS the printed uppers as safe; the short displays
0.346766 and 0.321864 of an earlier note are approximations, not upper bounds (AT-300). These are necessary conditions
for one conditional sufficient test; physical rates, weak coefficients and closure are OPEN.

## NR-14. T1-Rule-HFQcap at P-S1: center-independent ceilings of the HF-Q source norm (PROVED: AT-303, AT-304)

Hypotheses: q = 21/10, sigma = 1, r0 = 1/2 (P-S1, W = (1 + 4|X|^2)^(1/2)) and c = c_l; a normalized background that
satisfies the retained hypotheses of HF-NEC (AT-211, NR-7) and of the transferred AT-229 (4) closure framework (the
full inverse, domain, base-energy and common-center hypotheses; AT-303), and for which the HF-Q direct test of NR-10
passes at P-S1 with rate b0 > 0 and some C0 >= 0, R > 0, M > 0.
Constants (AT-303). sup |ell| = sigma/(2 r0) = 1 for ell = grad log W; L_t = r0^(-1-t) sigma 2^t Gamma((t + 1)/2)
Gamma((t + 3)/2), so L_0 = pi, L_1 = 8 and L_q = 45.0155497712569...; the CE coefficients are multiplied by
r0^(s-q). The R-free part of the refined loss is (c_B S_1 + B S_w sigma/(2 r0)) K with c_B = 53/100 and B > 1/2 for
every admissible pair (S_1, S_w and the pairs depend on q only).
Statement (original test, K = c_W M). With lambda_0 = c_W (c_B S_1 + S_w/2),
    N_(21/10,1,1/2)(F) < (c + 1 - q)^2/(4 lambda_0).
For c_W = 9619/5000 (AT-170): lambda_0 = 1.2331146376749650698... and the ceiling is 0.102292472444104... < 0.1022925.
For c_W = 1927/1000 (AT-135): lambda_0 = 1.2351657692066003168... and the ceiling is 0.102122604300969... < 0.1021227.
AT-303 records that both values of c_W are confirmed homogeneous constants. The two original tests are different
tests with different ceilings (AT-304).
Statement (CE test). If H5 is replaced by H5' of the second theory agent's Corollary HF-Q-CE, transferred to P-S1 (AT-303), then
    N_(21/10,1,1/2)(F) < (c + 1 - q)^2/(4 lambda_1) < 0.1967903,  lambda_1 = c_B S_1 + S_w/2 = 0.64097860363601...
Proof. As in NR-11, with the P-S1 constants. The base energy with rate b0 and finite C0 gives b0 <= beta <= c + 1 - q
(NR-7 (a); the origin entry does not depend on the weight, since ell(0) = 0). The loss exceeds lambda_0 M for the
original input since B > 1/2 and sup |ell| = 1. For the CE input, L_B(R, K) > lambda_1 K and every uniform Kbar is at
least M, by the normalized packet obstruction, which transfers to P-S1 (AT-303). NR-10 then gives
N_F < b0^2/(4 lambda) <= (c + 1 - q)^2/(4 lambda). QED (AT-303 for the transfer, AT-304 for the formulas and
decimals).
Rate form (AT-304). The test fails at every R > 0 and every C0 >= 0 when b0 <= sqrt(4 lambda N_F). At the AN-089
lowers the CONFIRMED detailed thresholds for c_W = 1927/1000 are 0.6932675 (N_F >= 0.097278426) and 0.5627286
(N_F >= 0.064093338); AT-304 REFUTED the coarse upward roundings 0.69327 and 0.56273 as safe thresholds.
At the AT-334 lowers (N_F > 0.160177521 for C5 + b7, N_F > 0.103720469 for 007 + b7), the same formula gives, rounded
down (the first theory agent research/qwg/v02/hfq_ps1/necr_at334/rate_thr_at334.out, 50-digit arithmetic with the lower decimal
lambda_1 = 0.64097860363601; CLAIMED, not audited): CE input 0.6408443 (C5 + b7) and 0.5156844 (007 + b7). Both
original-input thresholds exceed the cap c + 1 - q = 0.7103192... at both centers. These thresholds were CONFIRMED
in AT-343. At the AT-351 lowers (N_F > 0.231239122 for C5 + b7, N_F > 0.149564725 for 007 + b7) the CE test is
excluded through the radius lower (Uses), and no rate threshold is needed.
Scope. The ceilings hold for every center in the class, every R and every C^opt(b0); they limit route Q at P-S1 only
and are not nonexistence statements. Being below a ceiling is only necessary (AT-271, AT-303).
Uses. All of the following concern the stated sufficient tests under the origin-cap hypothesis b0 <= c + 1 - q. The
radius statements also need a positive signed gap beta_0 = c + 1 - theta_0 - kappa_0 with theta_0, kappa_0 >= 0 and
R >= ||F||_2/beta_0.
- Original input. The AT-351 P-S1 lowers 0.231239122 (C5 + b7) and 0.149564725 (007 + b7) both exceed the
  radius-free ceiling 0.1022925, as the AT-334 lowers 0.160177521 and 0.103720469 already did. The original-input test
  is therefore excluded at both registered b7 centers at every R (AT-335, AT-352). Earlier, radius-dependent
  exclusions used the second theory agent's Proposition NEC-R (AT-307) and certified radius lowers (AT-316, AT-317, AT-324).
- CE input. AT-351's unweighted lowers ||F||_2 > 7.65e-7 (C5 + b7) and > 4.90e-7 (007 + b7) give R > 1e-7 for every
  positive gap, and the NEC-R envelope at R = 1e-7 is below 0.141102. Both P-S1 lowers exceed that level. The CE test
  is therefore excluded at C5 + b7 (AT-335, already at the AT-334 lower) and at 007 + b7 (AT-352). For 007 + b7 the
  margin is a factor 1.0599 in the norm and 1.1235 in the energy (0.0223696 against 0.141102^2 = 0.019909774404).
  The numerical auditor recomputed the lowers and both envelopes independently (OBS-OUTER-DISK-001).
- Version 6 stated a necessary window for the CE test at 007 + b7 (R < 4.6e-4, b0 > 0.5156844, N_F < 0.141102;
  CONFIRMED in AT-343 as necessary conditions only). It is superseded at that source by AT-352.
- With AT-260, AT-274 and AT-287 at P-S2, the five named route-Q tests are excluded at both registered b7 centers,
  each under its retained hypotheses (AT-352). This is not a nonexistence result. For a new center, NR-14 and NEC-R
  give the screen: a certified P-S1 lower above 0.1967903 excludes every test of this form at every radius, and a
  certified lower above the NEC-R envelope at a certified radius lower excludes the CE test at that center.

## NR-15. T1-Rule-SecBud: sector budget rule for the weighted L^2 tail (PROVED: AT-311, AT-320)

Status: AT-311 CONFIRMED the conditional rule and formula; AT-320 CONFIRMED the corrected allowances and thresholds.

Hypotheses: (q, sigma, r0) is P-S2 = (21/10, 7/5, 1) or P-S1 = (21/10, 1, 1/2) (the proof uses only sigma >= 0 and
r0 > 0), W = (1 + r^2/r0^2)^(sigma/2), R > 0, and on r >= R
    F = sum_(k=1..K) r^(a_k) g_k(theta) + F_rem,  kappa_k = 2 a_k + 2 sigma + 2 < 0,  C_k = ||g_k||_(L^2(S^1)).
Statement.
    ||W F 1_(r >= R)||_2 <= (1 + r0^2/R^2)^(sigma/2) r0^(-sigma) sum_k C_k R^(a_k+sigma+1)/sqrt(-2(a_k+sigma+1))
                           + ||W F_rem 1_(r >= R)||_2.
If the g_k have pairwise disjoint angular frequency supports, the sum over k may be replaced by the root of the sum
of the squares.
Proof. On r >= R, W(r)^2 = (r/r0)^(2 sigma) (1 + r0^2/r^2)^sigma <= (r/r0)^(2 sigma) (1 + r0^2/R^2)^sigma, so the
energy of the k-th term is at most (1 + r0^2/R^2)^sigma r0^(-2 sigma) C_k^2 R^kappa_k/(-kappa_k) (AT-295). Minkowski's
inequality gives the display; for disjoint frequency supports the terms are orthogonal on every circle. QED (AT-311).
Allowances (AT-320). The largest circle norm of one sector whose tail upper bound beyond R is at most 0.01, rounded
down, is at R = 1e4: 0.322 (e = 4/c - 5), 2.75 (-3), 3.66 (a8 = -3.02850...) and 29.1 (a41 = -3.23806...) at P-S2,
and 9.12, 70.7, 93.2 and 704 at P-S1 (research/farfield_budget/FF_BUDGET_v04.md, SHA256 0f6d6782eeb9..., section 2,
with R = 1e3 to 1e8). For one sector of circle norm C = 1e7 the upper bound beyond R = 1e4 is at most 0.01 if and
only if a <= -4.57030612675... (P-S2) or a <= -4.24375677738... (P-S1); the half-line equivalence holds for R >= 1
(AT-320).
Scope. The rule bounds the weighted L^2 tail only. An upper bound of N_q(F) needs in addition the derivative terms and
a localization of J^q. A finite-radius certificate needs a proved bound of F_rem with its validity domain (AT-290,
AT-302; the numerical auditor's conditional remainder methods, AT-313 to AT-315, AT-329 to AT-331, have no candidate constants yet).
Meeting the allocated budgets together with a complete remainder bound is sufficient for a weighted L^2 tail target;
exact cancellation is one option, and neither is necessary (AT-311 REFUTED the asserted necessity of exact
cancellation with a nonzero coefficient below its allowance). For the truncated two-term mode-3 model of
the registered b7 sources, D3 alone over the pair lies strictly between 2.970265 and 8.829886 and D3 removed over the
pair strictly between 3.498693 and 8.787727, over the twelve sampled inputs (both sources, both weights,
R in {1e4, 1e5, 1e6}); continuous-radius and complete-source bounds are OPEN (AT-320).

## NR-16. T1-Rule-H4F: Fourier-L^1 and Sobolev forms of the high-frequency defect bounds (PROVED: AT-342)

Status: AT-342 CONFIRMED both parts under the stated Fourier and Sobolev hypotheses. Coefficient bounds for an actual
background, H4, the inverse and closure are OPEN. The identities (I), (II) and the bounds (III), (IV) used here are
the theory auditor's AT-332 and AT-333 (research/h4_principal_identities/PROOF.md); they are imported, not re-proved.
Setting (that of AT-332, AT-333). L^2(R^2; C) with f^(xi) = integral exp(-i x.xi) f(x) dx and inverse factor
(2 pi)^(-2). J = (1 - Delta)^(1/2), n = J^(-1), m = |D| J^(-1), R_j with symbol i xi_j/|xi|, omega(xi) = xi/|xi|.
S is bounded, measurable and pointwise Hermitian on L^2(R^2; C^2); a is bounded and real. The inner products are real
parts. v (vector) and g (scalar) are in L^2 with v^ and g^ vanishing on |xi| < Lambda, Lambda > 0.
Hypotheses. S = S0 + S1 and a = a0 + a1 with constant S0, a0 and S1^, a1^ in L^1. No second moment is assumed.
Statement (a). With kS(zeta) = min(|zeta|^2/(1 + Lambda^2)^2, arctan(1/Lambda)^2) and
ka(zeta) = min(|zeta|^2/Lambda^2, 4),
    |<S m v, m v> - <S v, v>| <= [ ||S||_(L^inf,op)/(1 + Lambda^2)
                                   + (1/2)(2 pi)^(-2) integral kS(zeta) ||S1^(zeta)||_op dzeta ] ||v||_2^2,
    |sum_j <a R_j g, R_j g> - <a g, g>| <= (1/2)(2 pi)^(-2) integral ka(zeta) |a1^(zeta)| dzeta ||g||_2^2.
Both right sides are finite and tend to 0 as Lambda tends to infinity. When the second moments M2(S) and M2(a) of
AT-333 are finite, each right side is at most the corresponding right side of (III) or (IV).
Statement (b). If s > 3 and J^s S1 is in L^2 with the Frobenius norm, then
    M2(S) <= ||J^s S1||_(2,Frobenius)/sqrt(2 pi (s - 1)(s - 2)(s - 3)),
and the same holds for a1 with |.| in place of the Frobenius norm. If M2(a) is finite, then ||Delta a1||_inf <= M2(a).
For s = 4 the factor is 1/sqrt(12 pi).
Proof (summary; AT-342 has the complete proof, research/h4_fourier_refinement/PROOF.md in the theory auditor). For r, s >= Lambda
the point (m, n)(r) lies on the unit arc at angle arctan(1/r), so the squared chord is at most arctan(1/Lambda)^2 and,
by AT-333, at most |xi - eta|^2/(1 + Lambda^2)^2. Two unit vectors omega(xi), omega(eta) are at distance at most 2, and
by the polar identity of AT-333 at most |xi - eta|/Lambda. Inserting the two minima in the exact Fourier kernels of
the double commutators and applying Young's inequality and Plancherel gives (a); dominated convergence gives the
limit. For (b), ||S1^||_op <= ||S1^||_Frobenius, Cauchy-Schwarz and
integral over R^2 of |zeta|^4 (1 + |zeta|^2)^(-s) dzeta = 2 pi/((s - 1)(s - 2)(s - 3)) give the bound; the Laplacian
bound follows by Fourier inversion. QED (AT-342).
Check (AT-342). For a1 = exp(-|x|^2/2): M2(a) = 2 = ||Delta a1||_inf and ||J^4 a1||_2^2 = 65 pi, so (b) gives
2.33 >= 2.
Scope (AT-342). A Fourier-L^1 function vanishes at spatial infinity, so a continuous coefficient with a nonconstant
angular limit at infinity is not a constant plus such a function; (a) does not apply to it. An approach to an angular
limit does not by itself determine a pointwise Fourier singularity. A nonzero two-sided profile r^(-gamma) with
0 < gamma <= 1 is not in L^2(R^2), but an upper bound O(r^(-gamma)) alone does not decide L^2 membership. The bounds
control the high-frequency part above Lambda only; the low-frequency part and the choice of Lambda belong to the H4
argument (the second theory agent's notes/T2_H4_v01.md, CLAIMED; Lemma HI' there uses the plain moments of AT-333).

## NR-17. T1-Prop-MA: Mellin-angular reduction of the T_II form for a degree-zero coefficient (PROVED: AT-487, AT-488)

Status. The identity and the fiber formula are CONFIRMED (AT-487; proof research/mellin_audit/PROOF.md of the theory auditor). The
tau = 0 values are CONFIRMED as whole-plane lowers (AT-488). The sampled log-radius spectrum is not a continuum bound
(AT-489, REFUTED for that inference). Its use for an actual far coefficient is OPEN (AT-490). Code:
the first theory agent research/osc_mellin/mellin_v1.py (FLOAT tables).

Statement. Let A(theta) be a real bounded angular coefficient (degree zero in X) with Fourier coefficients Ahat_n, and
Q_A(g) = sum_(j=1,2) <A R_j g, R_j g> - <A g, g> on L^2(R^2). Write R = R1 + i R2 and Rbar = R1 - i R2. The operator
S = (R* A R + Rbar* A Rbar)/2 - A commutes with dilations. On the Mellin fiber tau (basis e^(i n theta) r^(-1 + i tau))
R maps mode n to n + 1 with the unimodular factor c_n(tau) and Rbar maps n to n - 1 with d_n(tau), where
    c_n = -i i^(|n+1| - |n|) G_|n|/G_|n+1|,  d_n = -i i^(|n-1| - |n|) G_|n|/G_|n-1|,
    G_k(tau) = Gamma((k + 1 + i tau)/2)/Gamma((k + 1 - i tau)/2),
and the fiber matrix is S(tau)_(mn) = Ahat_(m-n) [ (conj(c_m) c_n + conj(d_m) d_n)/2 - 1 ]. Then
    sup_(g != 0) Q_A(g)/||g||_2^2 = sup_tau sup spec S(tau)   (AT-487),
and exact finite angular compressions of S(tau) give lower bounds. For A = cos(k theta) the top value of S(0) is
1/sqrt(2) for k = 1 and 1 for every integer k >= 2 (AT-488); these are lowers of the left side, and maximality over
tau is not proved.

Use. A whole-plane far price for a degree-zero model coefficient reduces to a family of explicit matrices. For the
actual far coefficient of 009 the reduction gives no upper until uniform tau and angular-tail bounds and the
coefficient errors are supplied (AT-490).

## NR-18. T1-Rule-Lev(l): necessary levels as functions of a certified source lower (PROVED: AT-519, AT-521)

Status. Monotonicity CONFIRMED with the full necessary-envelope hypotheses (AT-519). Every printed cell of
the first theory agent research/qwg/v02/hfq_ps1/necr_b14s/levels_vs_l.out CONFIRMED conditionally (AT-521). The chord rule is the theory auditor's
parameter lemma (AT-520), cited, not claimed here.

Statement. For the HF-Q necessary envelope (CE or original input, P-S1 or P-S2) with R = l/(c + 1), l a certified
lower of ||F_*||_2, the necessary level of N(F_*) is nonincreasing in l and nondecreasing in the rate b. So a source
lower may be rounded down to a table row and a rate up to a column; the opposite roundings are not justified (AT-519).
Between l = 1e-6 and 1e-5 the four columns b >= 0.60 drop by 4.357 to 4.4615 percent at P-S1 and 3.937 to 3.989
percent at P-S2 (AT-522). With the chord lemma (AT-520), chords between strict tabulated uppers are strict uppers.

## NR-19. T1-Prop-TII0-RHO: the TII-0 construction priced over every cutoff radius (PROVED under its premises: AT-549)

Status. The pointwise proposition and the cell rule CONFIRMED conditionally (AT-549; the theory auditor research/tii0_radius_audit/
PROOF.md), with the AT-552 wording corrections; the U cells of the scans CONFIRMED (AT-550, AT-557); end-rate repairs
CONFIRMED conditionally (AT-558); the scan decisions are FLOAT observations (AT-551, AT-559); the wording "no budget
for b0 < 0.20 at n = 0.01" REFUTED and corrected (AT-560). The exclusion for an actual source is OPEN.
Text, proof and FLOAT scans: the first theory agent research/tii0_far/rho_scan.md.

Statement (short form; the full text with its premises is rho_scan.md). At P-S1 let a TII-0 construction of
the second theory agent's notes/T2_NELO_v01.md with parameters (rho, mu, nu, t) satisfy e_II <= cap - b0, and put K = 32 phi c,
S(rho, b0) = cap - b0 - O2(rho) with O2 the far oscillation outside 2 rho, A_+- the near weights on |X| <= rho and L_+
the L^1 mass of atil_+ there. Then S > 0, mu^2 >= K/S and P_II >= 2 (1 + K/S)^q A_-(rho); if L_+(rho) >= pi S^2/K, then
P_II >= 2 (1 + K/S)^q (A_-(rho) + 2 A_+(rho)). Under the AT-408 premises and Corollary BUD-NEC the construction is
excluded at (rho, b0) whenever S <= 0 or the lower is at least U. A finite family of radius cells and rate intervals
decides every (rho, b0) at once (cell rule).

Use (FLOAT reading, theory_v09 V9-4). The exclusion of the whole family depends on the pair (l, n): the threshold n
lies between 0.0075 and 0.01 at l = 2.76e-6 and between 0.022 and 0.03 at l = 1.5e-6.

## NR-20. T1-Lemma-KB: the RC-10 reference-ball margin from the RC-5 margin at P-S1 (PROVED: AT-598)

Status. CONFIRMED (AT-598; the theory auditor research/kball_margin_audit/PROOF.md). The 60 displayed losses CONFIRMED at the
stipulated inputs, with the continuous minimum at the main radius in (0.15031319457946938, 0.15031319457946940)
(AT-599). The G2 bridge reduction that uses it is CONFIRMED in its explicit conditional form (AT-600). Text and
evaluation: the first theory agent research/recenter_g2/KBALL.md, kball_margin3.py and kball_margin3.out.

Statement. P-S1, N(k) = ||J^q (W k)||_2, Kbar = {k real S1: ||k||_2 <= Rb, N(k) <= Mb}. For all A, B > 8q,
    sup_(k in Kbar) sup_X lambda_max Sym DU[k](X) <= S(A) = Rb A^2/(8 sqrt(2 pi)) + mu(A) Mb (I3(A)/(32 pi))^(1/2),
    sup_(k in Kbar) sup_X |U[k](X)| <= V(B) = Rb B (3/(32 pi))^(1/2) + mu(B) Mb (3 I1(B)/(16 pi))^(1/2),
with I3(A) = int_A^inf r^3 (1 + r^2)^(-q) dr, I1(B) = int_B^inf r (1 + r^2)^(-q) dr, mu(A) = 1/(1 - 4q/A) +
e^(-A/4) [U^3 + 6 U^2 + 24 U + 48], U = 1 + A/2. Consequently betaK_* >= beta* - q S(A) - (sigma/(2 r0)) V(B).

Use. At Mb = 1/2 and Rb <= 2.8476e-6/(7/4) the loss is below 0.150314 (AT-599), so RC-10 on such a ball follows from
the RC-5 margin at every rate b0 >= 0.150314 at which the HF-Q test is applied. Application to a center also needs a
certified ||F_*||_2 upper and the RC-5 margin (G2-3, G2-4 of theory_v09 V9-5).

## NR-21. T1-Lemma-HS: the sign-aware far upper, general form (PROVED conditionally: AT-664, AT-706, AT-756)

Status. Case (O) is CONFIRMED under the stated hypotheses with a finite Schur cost (AT-664; the theory auditor
research/hessian_schur_audit/PROOF.md). The row-wise form HS-R is CONFIRMED conditionally (AT-706). The general
statement with cases (O) and (B), the row-wise form and the partition consequence are CONFIRMED (AT-756; the theory auditor
research/theory_v10_lemma_audit/PROOF.md). Case (B) with the clamped cosine and sine cutoffs is Lemma HSB of the second theory agent
(AT-695). Text and proof: the first theory agent research/far_hh/HS_DERIVATION.md, sections 2, 3 and 4.

Statement. Let a be real and bounded with M0 + M2 = (2 pi)^-2 int (1 + |zeta|^2) |a^| d zeta finite. Let
phi, theta : [0, infinity) -> [0, 1] be measurable and h > 0 measurable. Put v = J^-1 g,
Ktil = <xi><eta> |w - w'|^2 + (<xi>^q - <eta>^q)^2 (<xi><eta>)^(1-q) and
P = |zeta|^2 (w.w') + (q^2 - 1)(zeta.w)(zeta.w'). Take (O) L = |w - w'|^2, Lambda = osc(a), or (B) theta = 0
beyond K', L = Ktil, Lambda = A_low(K') (the continuum band constant of the complete far form). With
r_L = Ktil - phi phi' P - theta theta' L and the Schur row rho_h(xi) = (2 pi)^-2 int (1/2) |a^(xi - eta)| |r_L|
h(eta)/h(xi) d eta, if S_h = ess sup rho_h is finite, then
  B_a(g) <= ess sup_xi [SC(a) phi(|xi|)^2 + Lambda theta(|xi|)^2 + rho_h(xi)] ||v||^2
         <= SC(a) ||Phi v||^2 + Lambda ||Theta v||^2 + S_h ||v||^2,
and if phi^2 + theta^2 <= 1 the constant is at most max(SC(a), Lambda) + S_h. Here SC(a) is the Hessian floor
sup_x max(0, (1/2)[(q^2 - 1) lambda_max Hess a + Delta a]) of Lemma SC-LB.

Corrections and scope:
- AT-666: the rows need not be Lipschitz, and M2 alone does not make S_h finite.
- AT-758: the HSB remainder vanishes on the low-low block only.
- AT-759: a sup norm and M2 of a coefficient difference do not bound the change of S_h.
- AT-760: a double cutoff in the third instance.
- AT-761: the low-pass route has no universal osc floor.
- AT-805 REFUTED reading osc(b) as an upper of A_low(K').
- AT-806: A_low(K') <= OMF(b), or A_low(K') <= (1 + K'^2) osc(b) + C with C an upper of the C_aw[b] part, for
  real b with b^ = a^ on |zeta| <= 2K'.
- AT-797 to AT-799: power-weight conditions for a finite S_h.
- AT-697, AT-709: a periodic box value is not A_low(K').

Use. On FLOAT inputs this is the far side of theory_v10 V10-1. For the actual 009 and 013 far pieces no far upper is
certified (AT-680, AT-811, OPEN).

## NR-22. T1-Prop-HF1NEAR: the HF-1 near price with the combined form (PROVED conditionally: AT-691)

Status. Both inequalities CONFIRMED under N1 to N3 with mu >= 1, 2 nu <= mu, h in L^2 and the four finite weighted
kernel norms (AT-691; the theory auditor research/hf1_near_audit/PROOF.md). The floor of Remark 2 is CONFIRMED within the
construction (AT-692). It is not a lower bound of C^opt and excludes no other construction. Tail B is CONFIRMED under
the same hypotheses (AT-757). The three-piece split of the Riesz multiplier is an algebraic identity (AT-693). A
complete numerical price is OPEN (AT-693, AT-694). Text: the first theory agent research/hf1_near/HF1_NEAR.md, sections 2, 3 and 5.

Statement (short form; the hypotheses N1 to N3 are in HF1_NEAR.md section 2). For the near coefficient a = a_n,
every t in (0, 1) and every h with g = J^q W h in L^2,
  (TII + C_aw)[a](g) <= e_HF ||g||^2 + P_HF ||h||^2,
  e_HF = (1 + q^2) Lambda_+(a)/(2 mu^2) + (pi^2 (1 + c_aw/2) + C_D/2) M4(a)/mu^4 + tail + t^-1 pi^-1 ||a_+||_1 nu^2,
  P_HF = 2^sigma [ sup(|a| W^2) kappa_0^Psi(mu) kappa_2q^Psi(mu)
                 + (1 - t)^-1 sup(a_+ W^2) sum_j kappa_j^Psi(nu, mu)^2 ],
with tail A = 2^q T_q(a; mu/2) or tail B = (4/3)^q T_q(a; 3 mu/4) + (8/mu)^q T_q(a; mu/2).

Use. This is the near side of theory_v10 V10-2 and V10-4. The price tables are FLOAT and HEURISTIC: they use the 009
near inputs, a weight-part proxy for T_q and sampled M4. At the 013 budgets every computed split with rho <= 1 passes
the cap-level near test e_HF <= c + 1 - q - b0 on these values. In the H4 join the budget is beta_* - b0 - eps_rest
with a certified whole-plane matrix rate beta_* <= c + 1 - q (AT2333; the cap-level reading is REFUTED as a
deduction, AT2334), so a pass of the cap-level test is necessary, not sufficient, for the H4 step. At the 009 budgets
only erflog 8 at rho 0.6 and 0.5 pass the cap-level test (theory_v10 V10-6 (d)). Nothing is certified. Version 12:
the low cut may be any radial psi in L^2 (NR-27), and the four kernel norms of N3 have directed upper bounds by
NR-28; the 013 prices with them are in theory_v12 V12-1 (HEURISTIC).

## NR-23. T1-Lemma-KTB: a translation majorant of the far kernel (PROVED: AT-845; universal origin gain REFUTED: AT-846)

Status. The inequality is CONFIRMED for every q >= 1 and every choice of unit vectors at the origin, together with its
sharpness in the limit, and the majorant g_kappa below is admissible in AT-753 with constant 1 (AT-845; the theory auditor
research/translation_majorant_audit/PROOF.md). The accepted hypotheses of AT-753/754 and every recomputed integral are
still required. The version 9 sentence "Near zeta = 0 this lowers the origin and diagonal error terms by a factor of
about C/2 = 1.1e4 at B = 8 pi" is REFUTED as a general statement (AT-846) and is withdrawn. AT-847 CONFIRMED the
polynomial-cell extension conditionally. AT-848 (a source-specific certificate) is OPEN.

Statement. For q >= 1, all xi, eta in R^2 and any unit choices at the origin,
  Ktil(xi, eta) <= kappa(s) = (2 + s)^2 + q^2 s^2 (1 + s)^(q-1),   s = |xi - eta|,
with equality in the limit xi, eta -> 0 from opposite directions.

Proof. Put u = |xi|, v = |eta|, L = <xi>, l = <eta>. Since (1 + u v + |u - v|)^2 >= (1 + u v)^2 + (u - v)^2 =
L^2 l^2, one has L l <= 1 + u v + |u - v|. With |w - w'|^2 <= 4, |u - v| <= s and u v |w - w'|^2 = s^2 - (u - v)^2,
the angular term satisfies L l |w - w'|^2 <= 4 + 4 s + s^2. For the radial term assume L >= l (it is symmetric) and
put t = L/l. It equals l^2 (t^q - 1)^2 t^(1-q) <= q^2 (L - l)^2 t^(q-1) by the mean value theorem. Here
L - l <= s (t -> <t> is 1-Lipschitz) and t <= 1 + s (l >= 1). QED.

Use (corrected). In the finite matrix certificate of AT-753/754 for A_low(B), the kernel majorant
g_kappa(zeta) = min(C, kappa(|zeta|)/2) |a^(zeta)| on |zeta| <= 2B, used with constant 1, may replace C |a^|, where
C = R^(q+1) + R^2 and R = <B>.
- What holds for every coefficient. The diagonal term satisfies Ediag_new <= min(1, kappa(d)/(2C)) Ediag_old for the
  diagonal disk of radius d, with the limit 2/C as d -> 0 (AT-846).
- What depends on the coefficient. The origin term Eorigin is built from omega(eps), the largest mass of g in a
  translated eps-disk. It improves only where that mass sits at small |zeta|. AT-846 gives a coefficient whose
  spectral mass lies at |zeta| about 20, where kappa/2 > C. For it the two majorants coincide and Eorigin is
  unchanged.
- FLOAT reading for the 013 far coefficient at erflog 8, rho 1 (B = 8 pi, C = 2.2602e4; the first theory agent
  research/at_reviews/ktb_gain.py and ktb_gain_e8r1_N2048.out; grid sums, not bounds):
  - Eorigin falls by factors of 5.4e3 to 6.1e3 for eps 1e-4 to 0.05: 17.4 to 0.0032 at eps 1e-4 and 1786 to 0.33 at
    1e-2. The largest |a^| lies at |zeta| about 0.1.
  - Ediag at d = 0.1 falls from 4670 to 0.45.
  - The Schur bound of the whole kernel falls from C A0 = 3.04e4 to int g_kappa dmu = 10.6.
  These gains do not reduce the order of the matrix that a certificate needs (NR-24, Remark 2).
Review and sampling check: the first theory agent research/at_reviews/REVIEW_AT753_797_805.md (section 4 corrects its section 1.2) and
ktil_majorant_check.py. The second theory agent runs/check_kappa_t1n41_1135.out gives an independent check: 6 million pairs, largest ratio
0.99999999, attained only as s -> 0.

## NR-24. T1-Lemma-LPF: a floor for low-pass comparison coefficients (PROVED: AT-886, AT-887; FLOAT table AT-888; exclusion OPEN: AT-889)

Setting. a is a real bounded function on R^2. A comparison coefficient for a at band radius K' > 0 is a real bounded b
with b^ = a^ on the open disk |z| < 2K', in the sense of tempered distributions. The low-pass route of AT-806 bounds
A_low,a(K') = A_low,b(K') by OMF(b) = osc(b) + M2(b) + (q^2/2) M2[w_new](b), or by (1 + K'^2) osc(b) + C, where C is
an upper of C_aw[b] on the band. Here osc(b) = ess sup b - ess inf b.

Statement. Let 0 < K'' < K', let psi be a nonzero Schwartz function with psi^ in C_c^infinity supported in
|xi| <= K'', and put phi = |psi|^2/||psi||_2^2. Then every comparison coefficient b for a at K' satisfies
  phi * b = phi * a,   so   osc(b) >= osc(phi * a).
Consequently OMF(b) >= osc(phi * a), and (1 + K'^2) osc(b) + C >= osc(phi * a) for every admissible C. The floor of
the low-pass route at K' is therefore at least F(K') = sup osc(phi * a), the supremum taken over all such psi and
all K'' < K'.

Proof. phi is a nonnegative Schwartz function with int phi = 1. Its Fourier transform is (2 pi)^-2 ||psi||_2^-2 times
the convolution of psi^ with xi -> conj(psi^(-xi)) (the factor ||psi||_2^-2 was missing in version 10, AT-886), so
phi^ is in C_c^infinity with support in |z| <= 2K''. This is a compact subset of the open disk |z| < 2K'. For the
tempered distribution T = (b - a)^ and every test chi, <phi^ T, chi> = <T, phi^ chi> = 0, because phi^ chi is a test
function supported in |z| < 2K'. Since phi is Schwartz and b - a is tempered, (phi * (b - a))^ = phi^ T = 0, so phi *
b = phi * a. Since phi >= 0 and int phi = 1, (phi * b)(x) lies in [ess inf b, ess sup b] for every x. Hence osc(phi *
a) = osc(phi * b) <= osc(b). M2(b) and M2[w_new](b) are nonnegative, which gives the OMF statement.
For the second repair, every admissible C is nonnegative. Take S1 tests whose Fourier transforms are symmetrized
packets of radius delta at +-xi0 inside the band. Then J^-q f and J^q f equal <xi0>^-q f and <xi0>^q f up to
O(delta) in L2, so C_aw[b](f)/||J^-1 f||^2 -> 0 as delta -> 0. QED.

Remarks.
1. This is a floor of the comparison route only. It is not a lower bound of A_low(K'), and it is consistent with
   AT-761 (no universal oscillation floor for the band constant). It also covers the plain truncation b = a_(2K'),
   for which the second theory agent observed osc(a_(2K')) = osc(a_f) (T2_FAR_v02 Table O).
2. FLOAT reading for the windowed 013 far coefficient at erflog 8, rho 1 (the first theory agent research/far_cert/lpf_floor.py and
   lpf_floor_e8r1_N2048.out; N 2048, box 132; psi^(k) = exp(1 - 1/(1 - |k/K''|^2)) on |k| < K''; osc(a_f) = 0.7711):

   | K'' | 0.25 | 0.5 | 1 | 2 | 4 | 2 pi | 4 pi | 8 pi |
   |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
   | osc(phi * a_f) | 0.326 | 0.458 | 0.604 | 0.708 | 0.752 | 0.763 | 0.769 | 0.771 |

   So any comparison coefficient at K' > 2 pi has osc(b) of at least about 0.76 (FLOAT). The band room of Lemma HSB
   at this split and K' = 8 pi is 0.6290 (T2_FAR_v02 Table K) and Gbar = 0.6517. On these values the low-pass route
   of AT-806 cannot meet that room at 8 pi. The radius near K' = 1.2 at which the floor would cross 0.629 lies
   between the computed rows K'' = 1 (0.604) and 2 (0.708) and is interpolated, not computed; the room at other K'
   is not 0.6290 in general (AT-889: a certified exclusion is OPEN). This agrees with the second theory agent 11:32Z item 3 for the
   multiplier route (a). The band certificate must come from a finite enclosure with a projection error, or from the
   matrix route of AT-753/754.
3. Order of the matrix route (HEURISTIC). Consider tests concentrated on the support of the windowed coefficient
   (radius X = Rw + Ww = 60) and band-limited to |xi| <= K'. They have about X^2 K'^2/4 degrees of freedom: 5.7e5 at
   K' = 8 pi, or 1.4e5 after the parity factor 4. Cells or elements must resolve a^ on the scale 1/X, so a
   certificate at 8 pi needs at least this order. A smaller K' lowers the order by (K'/8 pi)^2, at the cost of a
   larger Schur cost S_h^B in Lemma HSB.

## NR-25. T1-Lemma-FTT: Fourier tails and moments from L2 derivative norms (PROVED: AT-890 to AT-892; no certified value yet: AT-893)

Purpose. HF1-NEAR (NR-22) needs certified values of M4(a_n) = (2 pi)^-2 int |zeta|^4 |a_n^| and of the tails
T_q(a_n; v) = (2 pi)^-2 int_(|zeta| > v) |zeta|^q |a_n^|. The sampled values are not usable: the sampled M4 is
dominated by an interpolation floor (the second numerics agent 11:34Z item 2), and the price tables use a model proxy for T_q (AT-778).
The lemma reduces both to L2 norms of derivatives of the exact near coefficient. Those norms can be enclosed by
interval quadrature of its closed form.

Statement. Let a be real on R^2 with a measurable Fourier transform a^ and finite norms below (for example a in
H^6, AT-891). For m >= 0 put ||D^m a|| = ((2 pi)^-2 int |zeta|^(2m) |a^(zeta)|^2 d zeta)^(1/2). For
an even integer m it is ||Delta^(m/2) a||_2, and for an odd integer m it is ||grad Delta^((m-1)/2) a||_2.
(i) For v > 0 and p >= 0 with m > p + 1:
      (2 pi)^-2 int_(|zeta| > v) |zeta|^p |a^(zeta)| d zeta <= v^(p+1-m) ||D^m a|| / (4 pi (m - p - 1))^(1/2).
(ii) For V > 0 and s > 1:
      M4(a) <= V (4 pi (s - 1))^(-1/2) ( (2 pi)^-2 int |zeta|^8 (1 + |zeta|^2/V^2)^s |a^(zeta)|^2 d zeta )^(1/2).
     For s = 2 the right side is V (4 pi)^(-1/2) (||D^4 a||^2 + 2 V^-2 ||D^5 a||^2 + V^-4 ||D^6 a||^2)^(1/2).
(iii) With D_k = ||D^k a|| and V^2 = D6/D4 (D4, D6 > 0), the s = 2 bound gives
      M4(a) <= ((D4 D6 + D5^2)/(2 pi))^(1/2) <= (D4 D6/pi)^(1/2).
     Certified uppers of D4, D5, D6 may be substituted (the right side is increasing in each).

Proof. (i) Write |zeta|^p |a^| = |zeta|^(p-m) |zeta|^m |a^| and apply Cauchy-Schwarz on |zeta| > v. The first factor
is int_(|zeta| > v) |zeta|^(2p-2m) d zeta = 2 pi v^(2p-2m+2)/(2m - 2p - 2). The second is (2 pi)^2 ||D^m a||^2.
Multiplying by (2 pi)^-2 gives (2 pi)^(-1/2) (2 (m - p - 1))^(-1/2) v^(p+1-m) ||D^m a||, which is the claim.
(ii) The same with the weight (1 + |zeta|^2/V^2)^(-s/2) and its inverse. Here
int (1 + |zeta|^2/V^2)^(-s) d zeta = pi V^2/(s - 1). The s = 2 expansion uses
(1 + x)^2 = 1 + 2x + x^2 with x = |zeta|^2/V^2.
(iii) At V^2 = D6/D4 the s = 2 bound squared is (V^2/(4 pi)) (D4^2 + 2 D5^2/V^2 + D6^2/V^4) = (2 D4 D6 + 2 D5^2)/(4 pi)
(AT-892). By Cauchy-Schwarz in zeta, D5^2 = (2 pi)^-2 int (|zeta|^4 |a^|)(|zeta|^6 |a^|) <= D4 D6, which gives the
second inequality. Monotonicity: each D_k enters with a nonnegative power. QED.

Use (HEURISTIC sizes). With p = q = 2.1 and m = 4, (i) gives T_q(a; v) <= 0.30 v^(-0.9) ||D^4 a||. With m = 6 it gives
0.17 v^(-2.9) ||D^6 a||. The tails of tail B are taken at 3 mu/4 and mu/2, about 31 and 20 at the erflog 8, rho 1 root
(mu 40.8, b0 0.55). They are useful only if ||D^m a_n|| is known well; the sampled field cannot supply it, for the
same reason as M4. The inputs ||D^m a_n|| (m = 4, 5, 6) are integrals of derivatives of the exact near coefficient
a_n = (1 - cut) a. They can be enclosed from its closed form: the exact 007 quotient base, the 013 correction atoms
of MANIFEST 615f09c5... and the erflog cut. This is the route the near owner table (theory_v11 V11-5, rows N4 and N5)
proposes for C(M4) and C(tail). No value is computed here. Targets (theory_v11 V11-5 (c); HEURISTIC, the HF1-NEAR
model with m = 6 in place of the tail proxy, solved at its own root): P_HF B <= D at f 7.97e-7, N 0.0055 needs D6 up
to about 5.28e4 at b0 0.55 and 4.27e4 at b0 0.60 at erflog 8, rho 1 (root mu about 75 and 80), and about 6.6e3 at log
16, rho 2 (b0 0.55). These are rounded planning targets, not downward-rounded thresholds; a certificate must use a
lower target recomputed in containing arithmetic (AT-969 OPEN).

## NR-26. T1-Prop-FA: a far upper without a band eigenvalue upper (PROVED conditionally: AT-911)

Setting. q = 21/10, J = (1 - Delta)^(1/2), the real S1 test space (Schwartz, even in x, odd in y), and the far form
B_a(g) = Q_a(g) + C_aw[a](g) with Q_a(g) = sum_j int a |R_j g|^2 - int a |g|^2 and C_aw[a](g) = int a |g|^2 - Re int a
(J^-q g)(J^q g). A_*(a) = sup B_a(g)/||J^-1 g||_2^2 over nonzero S1 tests. For the far piece a_f of a split and a
window radius Rw with width Ww, put a_W = a_f (1 - Sinf((r - Rw)/Ww)) (the windowed piece) and a_t = a_f - a_W (the
tail).

Statement. Let A_E^+ be a certified upper of the band constant of a_W on |xi| <= K' obtained from route (a) of
T2_FAR_v02 section 2.5 (absolute-kernel band rows; AT-707, AT-882), and let SC^+ and S^+ be certified inputs of
Lemma HSB (NR-21) for a_W at (K, K'). Let OMF^+(a_t) be a certified upper of osc(a_t) + M2(a_t) + (q^2/2)
M2[w_new](a_t), with a_t satisfying the hypotheses of the OMF baseline (AT-665: real, bounded, finite osc, M2 and
M2[w_new]). Then
    A_*(a_f) <= max(SC^+, A_E^+) + S^+ + OMF^+(a_t).
Consequently the far test A_*(a_f) <= q (c - s_*) holds whenever the certified strain upper satisfies
s_* <= c - (max(SC^+, A_E^+) + S^+ + OMF^+(a_t))/q.

Proof. Lemma HS (NR-21, AT-756) applied to a_W with the band upper A_E^+ gives B_(a_W)(g) <= (max(SC^+, A_E^+) + S^+)
||J^-1 g||_2^2 for every S1 test g; Lemma HS does not require A_E^+ <= SC^+ (AT-857). The OMF baseline gives
B_(a_t)(g) <= OMF^+(a_t) ||J^-1 g||_2^2 on the same test space and in the same norm. B_a is linear in a, so
B_(a_f) = B_(a_W) + B_(a_t). Add the two inequalities and take the supremum. No test-function localization enters
(AT-911). QED.

Remarks.
1. What it avoids and what it costs. No eigenvalue upper of a band matrix is needed: A_E^+ is an absolute-value
   integral of the windowed coefficient and its inputs are a majorant of |a_W^| and a modulus of continuity in xi
   (theory_v11 V11-4 (a), rows C3 and C4 of the second theory agent's owner table). The price is that A_E^+ lies above Gbar = 0.65167
   at erflog 8, rho 1 (the route (a) floor is osc(a_f) = 0.771, AT-882), so the strain target must be lower than 3/2.
   At s_* <= 137/125 the far value must not exceed G = 1.500070341420 (AT-935).
2. FLOAT table at erflog 8, rho 1 (013, K' 8 pi, K 2 pi, tau 1; the second theory agent 12:49Z and 12:55Z; theory_v11 V11-4 (a)). A_E
   1.1369 at Rw 50 and 1.1345 at Rw 100 (N 4096, AT-972); S_h^B 0.02269 (Rw 50; A_E + S = 1.15961868528 at Rw 50). The
   tail charge is the FLOAT OMF of a_t cut again at Ro = 4 Rw (width Ro/5); the part beyond Ro needs its own charge.

   | Rw | osc, M2, M2[w_new] of the cut tail; OMF | A | level c - A/q | margin to G |
   |---:|---|---:|---:|---:|
   | 50 | 0.2649, 0.1315, 0.1607; 0.7507 | 1.9103 | 0.9006 | -0.4103 |
   | 100 | 0.1889, 0.0228, 0.0244; 0.2656 | 1.4228 | 1.1328 | 0.0773 |
   | 200 | 0.1354, 0.0040, 0.0041; 0.1484 | 1.3080 | 1.1875 | 0.1921 |

   The Rw 100 row uses the Rw 100 A_E and the Rw 50 S; the Rw 200 row uses the Rw 50 A_E and S (not computed at Rw
   200). AT-970 replayed the arithmetic with the Rw 50 inputs (1.910332759386, 1.425170874173, 1.307974383342) and
   REFUTED the earlier sentence that 1.096 covers every row. With A_E at its floor 0.771 the Rw 50, Ww 10 row is
   still at least 1.52 > G, so Rw 50 with Ww 10 is excluded for this route on FLOAT values (version 12: the exclusion
   holds for that width and the oscillation-floor family only; AT-1012 permits Ww = Rw, where the floor gives only
   0.7711 + 0.2273 = 0.998 and the matched FLOAT row is 1.3842, margin 0.1159, the second theory agent 14:26Z). Every
   OMF of this table is the charge of a_t (1 - v_Ro), the tail cut again at Ro = 4 Rw; the exterior part a_t v_Ro is
   charged by none of the rows (AT-973 OPEN). Matched row (version 12; the second theory agent 13:23Z and 13:36Z): at Rw 200, Ww 40,
   A_E 1.1343 and S_h^B 0.0226 give A = 1.3053, level 1.1888 and margin 0.1948 to G (FLOAT; AT-1043, AT-1044
   CONFIRMED the inputs as finite and the arithmetic); at Rw 100, Ww 100 every input matched gives A = 1.3111, level
   1.1860 and margin 0.1890 (FLOAT; the second theory agent 14:26Z); at Rw 200, Ww 200, with S_h^B taken from Rw 50, Ww 10 because it
   is not computed there, A = 1.2664, level 1.2073 and margin 0.2337 (FLOAT; the second theory agent 14:50Z). None of the margins is
   certified slack; they must absorb the enclosure errors of every input and the exterior part of the tail.
3. The paired Schur estimate (AT-959) does not improve the tail charge: its constant for a_t is at least
   Wi(a_t) + M2 + (q^2/2) M2, recomputed by AT-1020 from the second theory agent's saved tail rows as 1.0576154 / 0.5246310 /
   0.3350601 at Rw 50 / 100 / 200 (FLOAT finite-box values, not bounds on the complete tail), above OMF(a_t). (The
   attribution of these values to the theory auditor audit_v300 in version 11 is withdrawn.)
4. Placement. The tail is charged outside the band (placement P2 of V11-4). Charging it inside the band (P1) needs
   SC^+ and S^+ of the unwindowed a_f and a band upper, which is what the route avoids.
5. Hypotheses at 013 (version 12). The qualitative coefficient hypotheses of the statement (Lemma HSB for a_W, the
   OMF baseline AT-665 for a_t) hold for the 013 far piece at the erflog and log cuts by the second theory agent's Theorems C5a, C5b
   and Corollary C5 (a_f smooth, even in x_1 and x_2 with 81 cosine modes, in S^(-lambda) with all moments finite;
   AT-1147 to AT-1150 CONFIRMED). The constants of the symbol class are not enclosed, so A_E^+, SC^+, S^+ and
   OMF^+(a_t) are not certified (AT-1152 OPEN).

## NR-27. T1-Prop-HF1NEAR-L2: HF1-NEAR with a radial L^2 low cut (PROVED conditionally: AT-1094, AT-1134)

Status. CONFIRMED under the hypotheses N1 to N3 of AT-691 with the original restrictions on nu and mu, for every
radial measurable psi in L^2(R^2) (complex values allowed), with the high kernels defined with the same psi (AT-1094;
the theory auditor research/n6_analytic_audit/PROOF.md, section "General low cut in HF1-NEAR"). For psi = exp(-|xi|^4) the
exact split, the finite cubic-weight kernel estimate and the coefficient 1/(8 sqrt(2 pi)) are CONFIRMED (AT-1134;
research/n6_quartic_kernel_replay/PROOF.md). Text: theory_v12 V12-1 (f) 1.

Statement. In NR-22 replace the low cut by a radial measurable psi in L^2(R^2), put psi_nu = psi(./nu), and define
kappa_j^Psi(nu, mu) = || w F^-1[omega_j (1 - psi_nu) Psi_mu <.>^q] ||_1 (j = 1, 2) with this psi, finite as part of
N3. Then the inequality of NR-22 holds with the term t^-1 pi^-1 ||a_+||_1 nu^2 of e_HF replaced by
  t^-1 ||a_+||_1 (2 pi)^-2 ||psi||_2^2 nu^2;
the other terms of e_HF and P_HF are unchanged.

Proof. Step 3(a) of HF1-NEAR uses the cut only through the two low kernels phi_j = F^-1[omega_j psi_nu], and
sum_j ||phi_j||_2^2 = (2 pi)^-2 ||psi_nu||_2^2 = (2 pi)^-2 nu^2 ||psi||_2^2, since sum_j |omega_j|^2 = 1 almost
everywhere. The convolutions of phi_j with an L^2 function are bounded pointwise by Cauchy-Schwarz, so psi need not
be a bounded multiplier, and the decomposition of the Riesz multiplier holds in the sense of distributions. Steps
3(b) and 4 use the high kernels only through the norms of N3. For psi supported in |xi| <= 2 with values in [0, 1],
||psi||_2^2 <= 4 pi, so (2 pi)^-2 ||psi||_2^2 <= 1/pi and the old term is recovered as a bound (AT-1094). QED.

Remarks.
1. For psi = exp(-|xi|^4), (2 pi)^-2 ||psi||_2^2 = 1/(8 sqrt(2 pi)) (EXACT; AT-1094). For psi = exp(-|xi|^2) it is
   1/(8 pi), but the high symbol omega_1 (1 - exp(-|xi/nu|^2)) <xi>^q has B_3 = infinity, so the bound of NR-28 (i)
   does not apply to it (AT-1095 REFUTED the opposite claim). The quartic cut has 1 - psi = |xi|^4 + O(|xi|^8), so
   B_0 to B_3 of its mode-1 symbol are finite.
2. Use (HEURISTIC). With psi = exp(-|xi|^4), nu = 1/40 and lam_k = 256/25 the directed kappa_j uppers of theory_v12
   V12-1 (f) 1 (UB; CONFIRMED, AT-1135) are lower than those with psi = Psi_1, nu = 1/100 by about 2788 at every
   grid mu, and P/D falls by 8 to 18 percent at erflog 8, rho 1. At log 16, rho 2 the near test then has a window at
   b0 0.55 and 0.60 in the cap-level form (in the H4 join the near rate budget is beta_* - b0 - eps_rest, AT2333,
   AT2334). The other near inputs are FLOAT and D is a CE budget at stipulated inputs; nothing here proves
   a near closure or a near window (AT-1097, AT-1137 OPEN).

## NR-28. T1-Lemma-KUB: directed upper bounds of the HF1-NEAR kernel constants (PROVED: AT-1091 to AT-1093, AT-1134)

Status. Parts (i) to (iv) CONFIRMED: (i) by AT-1091, (ii) by AT-1092, (iii) and (iv) by AT-1093 (the theory auditor
research/n6_analytic_audit/PROOF.md). Part (v) is CONFIRMED for psi = exp(-|xi|^4), with the finite kernel estimate
of its pieces, by AT-1134 (research/n6_quartic_kernel_replay/PROOF.md). The numerical intervals of the first theory agent
research/kernel_n6/ (kappa_ub.py, kappa_ub_g.py, runs/*.json, n6_display.py) are CONFIRMED: for psi = Psi_1,
nu = 1/100 at the ten grid mu, with the angular refinement, the telescoping and the upward-rounded displays (AT-1119
to AT-1121; research/n6_kernel_replay/PROOF.md), and for the quartic cut, nu = 1/40, lam_k = 256/25, the 17 bounds
and 10 displays (AT-1135). AT-1136 REFUTED the integer 17775 of note 47 as an outward rounding (the display is
17775.1). AT-1095 and AT-1096 REFUTED two uses (the Gaussian cut; a reading of larger uppers), not the method. Text:
theory_v12 V12-1 (b), (c) and (f) 1.

Statement. Let w(x) = (1 + 4|x|^2)^(1/2), F^-1 with the factor (2 pi)^-2, and P(t) = cos((pi/2) S(t)) the AT-420 step,
Psi_lam(xi) = P(|xi|/lam - 1).
(i) Weighted L^1 bound. Let m be real, m in H^3(R^2), K = F^-1 m, and V(x)^2 = c0 + c1 |x|^2 + c2 |x|^4 + c3 |x|^6
   with rational c_n > 0. Then
     ||w K||_1 <= (2 pi)^-1 (W (c0 B_0 + c1 B_1 + c2 B_2 + c3 B_3))^(1/2),
     W = pi int_0^inf (1 + 4u)/(c0 + c1 u + c2 u^2 + c3 u^3) du,
   with B_0 = int m^2, B_1 = int |grad m|^2, B_2 = int (Lap m)^2, B_3 = int |grad Lap m|^2. For m = n(rho) the
   radial densities of B_0 to B_3 are n^2 rho, n'^2 rho, (n'' + n'/rho)^2 rho and (n''' + n''/rho - n'/rho^2)^2 rho,
   with angular factor 2 pi; for m = cos(theta) n(rho), with L1 n = n'' + n'/rho - n/rho^2, they are n^2 rho,
   (n'^2 + n^2/rho^2) rho, (L1 n)^2 rho and ((L1 n)'^2 + (L1 n)^2/rho^2) rho, with angular factor pi. For a real
   mode-1 symbol the right side may be multiplied by 2 sqrt(2)/pi. Every fixed choice of the c_n is valid.
(ii) Lemma FE. Let 0 < d <= 1/100, phi = exp(-1/(1 - 3d/2)), w0 = pi exp(-2/(3d))/phi, b0 = (w0^2/2) cosh w0 and
   b1 = w0 cosh w0, and let kmax >= 1 with d <= 2/(3 kmax). Then |P(t) - 1| <= b0 and |P^(k)(t)| <= k! (2/d)^k b0
   for t <= d, and |P(t)| <= b1 and |P^(k)(t)| <= k! (2/d)^k b1 for t >= 1 - d (1 <= k <= kmax).
(iii) Telescoping. For lam_0 = nu < lam_1 < ... < lam_N = mu with lam_(i+1) >= 2 lam_i,
     (1 - Psi_nu) Psi_mu = sum_(i < N) (1 - Psi_(lam_i)) Psi_(lam_(i+1)),
   each piece is smooth with compact support, and kappa_j^Psi(nu, mu) with psi = Psi_1 is at most the sum of the
   bounds (i) of the pieces omega_j (1 - Psi_(lam_i)) Psi_(lam_(i+1)) <.>^q.
(iv) W integral. For 0 <= a <= u <= b, (1 + 4u)/V^2(u) <= (1 + 4b)/V^2(a); further
   int_0^u0 (1 + 4u)/V^2 du <= u0 (1 + 4 u0)/c0 and int_U^inf (1 + 4u)/V^2 du <= (1/(2U^2) + 4/U)/c3.
(v) Split for the cut of NR-27. For any psi and a grid point lam_k of (iii) with 2 lam_k <= mu,
     (1 - psi_nu) Psi_mu = (1 - psi_nu) Psi_(lam_k) + sum_(i >= k) (1 - Psi_(lam_i)) Psi_(lam_(i+1))
                           - psi_nu (1 - Psi_(lam_k)) Psi_mu.

Proof. (i) to (iv): AT-1091 to AT-1093 (Plancherel on smooth compactly supported symbols and H^3 approximation;
polar differentiation; one-dimensional Cauchy-Schwarz in r for K = i cos(theta) G(r); Cauchy's estimate on the disk
|z - t| <= t/2 with the monotonicity of t^-k exp(-2/(3t)) for t <= 2/(3k); Psi_(lam_(i+1)) = 1 wherever
Psi_(lam_i) is nonzero; monotonicity of the integrand and V^2 >= c0, V^2 >= c3 u^3). (v): Psi_mu = 1 on |xi| <= mu and
Psi_(lam_k) = 0 on |xi| >= 2 lam_k, so Psi_(lam_k) Psi_mu = Psi_(lam_k). Hence (1 - psi_nu) Psi_mu =
(1 - psi_nu) Psi_(lam_k) + (1 - Psi_(lam_k)) Psi_mu - psi_nu (1 - Psi_(lam_k)) Psi_mu, and
(1 - Psi_(lam_k)) Psi_mu = Psi_mu - Psi_(lam_k) is the telescoping sum of (iii) from i = k. QED.

Remarks.
1. Computation. The B_n are bounded by sums over subintervals of the width times an arb upper of the density, with
   the symbol derivatives enclosed by arb power series in centered form and, on the flat ends of the step, by (ii)
   with kmax 5. For the cut of NR-27 the low piece on [0, rho0], with rho0 the binary64 number float(nu)/1024 (not
   nu/1024; the two differ by 1/737869762948382064640 at nu = 1/40, and the first panel starts at the same endpoint,
   so the cover of [0, 2 lam_k] is unchanged; AT-1190), uses Taylor's formula: n(rho) =
   (1 - exp(-(rho/nu)^4)) <rho>^q has n^(k)(0) = 0 for k <= 3, so with M an upper of sup |n''''| there,
   |L1 n| <= (17/24) M rho^2 and |(L1 n)'| <= (23/12) M rho, and the four densities are integrated in closed form
   (restated in the theory auditor's proof file, section "Gaussian H3 obstruction and the quartic alternative").
2. Use (UB, CONFIRMED; HEURISTIC prices). The uppers of kappa_0, kappa_2q and kappa_j at mu in {41, 41.5,
   42, 45, 46, 48, 52, 56, 64, 80} are in theory_v12 V12-1 (c) and (f) 1. They raise the near prices of theory_v11 by
   a factor 1.6 to 1.7. A larger upper says nothing about the size of a smaller model value, and a sum of piece norms
   can exceed the norm of the sum (AT-1096); displayed decimals are consumed only after outward rounding. The near
   price windows built on these uppers stay HEURISTIC (AT-1122, AT-1137 OPEN).
3. For kappa_0, kappa_2q and the top piece [256/25, mu] of kappa_j, NR-29 gives smaller uppers; the low and
   correction pieces of (v) keep the bound (i) with the factor 2 sqrt(2)/pi.

## NR-29. T1-Lemma-KSPLIT: split bound for the HF1-NEAR kernel constants (PROVED: AT-1241, AT-1250)

Status. Part (i) with (iii) is CONFIRMED for the two mode-0 symbols by AT-1241 (the theory auditor
research/split_kernel_mode0_audit/PROOF.md), part (ii) with (iii) for the top mode-1 symbol by AT-1250
(research/split_kernel_mode1_audit/PROOF.md). The numerical uppers of the first theory agent research/kernel_n6/split_ub.py and
split_ub1.py at R0 = 16/mu and the ten grid mu are CONFIRMED by fresh 320-bit replays: the 20 mode-0 bounds and
their displays (AT-1242) and the ten top-piece bounds with the combined quartic-cut kappa_j uppers (AT-1251).
AT-1243 and AT-1252: these constants give no physical near bound, full inverse or existence (OPEN). Text: theory_v13
V13-1 (g); audit requests: the first theory agent's notes 49, 49a and 49b.

Statement. Let w, F^-1, P and Psi_lam be as in NR-28, q = 21/10, and V^2(u) = c0 + c1 u + c2 u^2 + c3 u^3 (u = |x|^2)
with rational c_n > 0. Let R0 > 0 and W_ext = pi int_(R0^2)^inf (1 + 4u)/V^2(u) du.
(i) Mode 0. Let m(xi) = Psi_mu(|xi|) <xi>^(2s) with s = 0 or s = 21/10, K = F^-1 m (radial, K = K(r)), and B_0 to
   B_3 the moments of NR-28 (i) for m. Then
     ||w K||_1 <= 2 pi int_0^R0 w |K| r dr
                  + W_ext^(1/2) ((2 pi)^-2 sum_n c_n B_n - 2 pi int_0^R0 V^2 K^2 r dr)^(1/2),
   where the second bracket is nonnegative.
(ii) Mode 1. Let 0 < lo <= hi/2, n(k) = (1 - Psi_lo(k)) Psi_hi(k) <k>^q and m(xi) = cos(theta) n(|xi|), with B_0 to
   B_3 the moments of NR-28 (i) for this mode-1 symbol (angular factor pi). Then K = F^-1 m = i cos(phi) G(r) with
   G(r) = (2 pi)^-1 int_0^inf n(k) J1(k r) k dk, and
     ||w K||_1 <= 4 int_0^R0 w |G| r dr
                  + 4 (W_ext/(2 pi))^(1/2) ((2 pi)^-2 sum_n c_n B_n/pi - int_0^R0 V^2 G^2 r dr)^(1/2).
   The factor 2 sqrt(2)/pi of NR-28 (i) is not applied to this bound.
(iii) Series and cell bounds. Write m(xi) = n(|xi|) in mode 0. With M_n = int n(k) k^(2n+1) dk in mode 0 and
   M_n = int n(k) k^(2n+2) dk in mode 1, and h = mu in mode 0 and h = hi in mode 1, one has 0 <= M_n <= (2h)^(2n) M_0,
     K(r) = (2 pi)^-1 sum_n (-1)^n (r/2)^(2n) M_n/(n!)^2,   G(r) = (2 pi)^-1 sum_n (-1)^n (r/2)^(2n+1) M_n/(n! (n+1)!),
   with absolute and uniform convergence on bounded r together with the derivative series, and
   |K''| <= (2 pi)^-1 M_1, |G''| <= (2 pi)^-1 (M_0 M_1)^(1/2).

Proof. (i) Split ||w K||_1 at |x| = R0. On |x| > R0, Cauchy-Schwarz with the weight V gives
int_(|x| > R0) w |K| <= ||w/V||_(L^2(|x| > R0)) ||V K||_(L^2(|x| > R0)); with u = r^2 the first factor squared is
W_ext, and ||V K||_2^2 = (2 pi)^-2 sum c_n B_n by Plancherel (NR-28 (i)), from which the disk part
2 pi int_0^R0 V^2 K^2 r dr is subtracted. (ii) The same with the angular integrals int_0^(2 pi) |cos phi| d phi = 4 and
int_0^(2 pi) cos^2 phi d phi = pi, so that ||V K||_(L^2(|x| > R0))^2 = pi int_(R0)^inf V^2 G^2 r dr and
pi int_0^inf V^2 G^2 r dr = (2 pi)^-2 sum c_n B_n. (iii) The symbols are smooth, nonnegative on the radius and
supported in k <= 2h; term-wise integration of the J0 and J1 series is justified by absolute convergence; |J_l| <= 1
on the real axis by the integral representation, J0'' = (J2 - J0)/2 and J1'' = (J3 - 3 J1)/4 give |J0''|, |J1''| <= 1,
and int n k^3 dk <= (M_0 M_1)^(1/2) by Cauchy-Schwarz with the measure n(k) dk. The theory auditor proofs restate each step
(AT-1241, AT-1250). QED.

Remarks.
1. Computation (CONFIRMED, AT-1242, AT-1251). The M_n are enclosed by ball integration with the flat ends of NR-28
   (ii) (d = 1/100); the series are summed to N = 100 with geometric tail bounds (h R0 = 16, consecutive ratios
   below 1/2); the inner integrals and the inner moments are bounded on 20000 radial cells with the first-order
   Taylor form and (iii); W_ext by NR-28 (iv). The c_n are fixed rationals chosen in FLOAT; no optimality is used.
2. Values (UB, CONFIRMED). At R0 = 16/mu the split uppers are 0.9242 to 0.9246 times the NR-28 uppers of kappa_0 and
   0.9035 to 0.9060 times those of kappa_2q; the top piece of kappa_j is 0.9110 to 0.9148 times its NR-28 upper with
   the angular factor, and kappa_j for the quartic cut (nu 1/40, lam_k 256/25) is 0.9138 to 0.9242 times its value
   in NR-28 Remark 2 (ten grid mu; at mu 83/2: kappa_0 <= 3.295431, kappa_2q <= 1.103677e8, top piece <= 12086.18,
   kappa_j <= 13685.8). The split uppers of kappa_0 and kappa_2q lie 0.6 and 1.3 percent above the direct FLOAT
   values at mu 83/2.
3. Use (HEURISTIC). With both splits the HF1-NEAR price P/D at the 013 inputs falls by about 15 percent against
   theory_v12 (theory_v13 V13-1 (g) 6, V13-0 item 1). The prices stay HEURISTIC, and the near inequality needs the
   near energies D4 to D6 and the other N inputs (AT-1243, AT-1252). The cut piece (1 - psi_nu) Psi_(lam_k) of
   kappa_j is not split.

## NR-30. T1-Lemma-CORE: the radial core of the near coefficient in M4 and the Fourier tails (PROVED: AT-1328)

Status. CONFIRMED by AT-1328 (the theory auditor research/radial_core_nr30_audit/PROOF.md) with these conventions: a_c is in L^2
but not in L^1, and a_c^ is its L^2 (tempered) transform, represented away from zero by the K0 formula of (i); D^m
is for integer m >= 0 (for real m the formula of (iii) defines the homogeneous Sobolev seminorm); in (iv) a and a_r
have compatible measurable Fourier representatives, values may be infinite, and each NR-25 bound used needs its own
hypotheses; T_p(a_r; v) is an upper allowance, not in general T_p(a; v) - T_p(a_c; v). AT-1329 CONFIRMS the exact
regional core energies and, from the accepted center-1 record, 92000 < E6 of the remainder < 195000 on
63/64 <= r <= 65/64 (a regional Laplacian energy statement). AT-1330 REFUTES the sentence of the audited Purpose
that the origin energies of orders 4 and 6 agree to 1e-9 relative (corrected below). AT-1331 reproduces the 21 rows
of research/kernel_n6/runs/n6s_core.out as floating-model calculations; none is a certified tail or threshold.
AT-1332: no whole-plane remainder norm, near or far closure, background bound, inverse or existence follows (OPEN).
After AT-1328: AT-1343 REFUTES the remainder a_n - a_c (a_n(0) = -c); AT-1363 CONFIRMS the origin jet of Remark 5,
a_n + a_c = 4(c - 1)(x_1^2 - x_2^2) + O(|x|^4), and AT-1364 the revised origin comparisons of the Purpose (2e-16,
5.7e-6 and 1.2e-9); AT-1365: no remainder norm or near closure follows (OPEN). AT-1348 CONFIRMS the Laplacian and
tensor disk fractions of Remark 1 (equal global energies) and AT-1349 REFUTES their tensor reading; AT-1350 OPEN
(the theory auditor research/signed_radial_core_audit, origin_core_expansion_audit, radial_core_local_energy_audit).

Purpose. NR-25 bounds M4(a_n) and the tails T_q(a_n; v) by L2 derivative norms of the whole near coefficient a_n.
Near the origin a_n agrees with -a_c, where a_c = c/(1 + 4|x|^2) is the radial core (a_n(0) = -c; Remark 5): on the
CONFIRMED origin pair r <= 1/4096 (AT-1283, AT-1284) the energies of orders 4, 5 and 6 agree with those of a_c to
2e-16, 5.7e-6 and 1.2e-9 relative (exact core energies against the CONFIRMED intervals;
research/near_core/core_energy.out; the audited text said 1e-9 at orders 4 and 6, REFUTED for order 6 by AT-1330,
whose value is 1.1268e-9). By (iii) below D6(a_c) = 43170.3 (FLOAT), which is above every m = 6 planning target
of theory_v13 V13-4 (b) 4. The core has an explicit positive Fourier transform, so its share of M4 and of the tails
is computed exactly, and NR-25 is needed only for the remainder a_n + a_c (Remark 5).

Statement. Let c > 0, r0 = 1/2 and a_c(x) = c/(1 + 4|x|^2) = c r0^2/(r0^2 + |x|^2) on R^2. The transform, the norms
||D^m .||, M4 and T_p are those of NR-25: a^(zeta) = int a(x) e^(-i x.zeta) dx, M4(a) = (2 pi)^-2 int |zeta|^4 |a^|
and T_p(a; v) = (2 pi)^-2 int_(|zeta| > v) |zeta|^p |a^|.
(i) a_c is in L^2(R^2) and a_c^(zeta) = 2 pi c r0^2 K0(r0 |zeta|) > 0 for zeta != 0 (K0 the modified Bessel
    function of order 0).
(ii) M4(a_c) = Delta^2 a_c(0) = 1024 c, and for v > 0 and p >= 0,
      T_p(a_c; v) = c r0^2 int_v^inf t^(p+1) K0(r0 t) dt.
(iii) For m >= 0, ||D^m a_c||^2 = 2 pi c^2 r0^(2-2m) int_0^inf u^(2m+1) K0(u)^2 du
      = 2 pi c^2 r0^(2-2m) pi^(1/2) Gamma(m + 1)^3 / (4 Gamma(m + 3/2)).
(iv) Let a be real on R^2 with a measurable transform, and put a_r = a - a_c. Then M4(a) <= 1024 c + M4(a_r) and
     T_p(a; v) <= T_p(a_c; v) + T_p(a_r; v); NR-25 (i) to (iii) apply to a_r whenever its norms are finite. Hence the
     tail-B combination (4/3)^q T_q(a; 3 mu/4) + (8/mu)^q T_q(a; mu/2) is at most the same combination for a_c plus
     that for a_r.

Proof. (i) a_c is bounded and decays like |x|^-2, so it is in L^2. The fundamental solution of r0^2 - Delta on R^2
is G(x) = (2 pi)^-1 K0(r0 |x|); G is in L^1 (logarithmic at 0, exponentially decaying) and G^(zeta) =
(r0^2 + |zeta|^2)^-1. For f in L^1 with f^ in L^2, the L^2 transform of f^ is (2 pi)^2 f(-.). With f = G this gives
that the transform of (r0^2 + |x|^2)^-1 is (2 pi)^2 G = 2 pi K0(r0 |zeta|); multiply by c r0^2.
(ii) |zeta|^4 a_c^ is in L^1, so Fourier inversion gives Delta^2 a_c(0) = (2 pi)^-2 int |zeta|^4 a_c^, which is
M4(a_c) because a_c^ > 0. In polar coordinates (2 pi)^-2 int |zeta|^4 a_c^ = c r0^2 int_0^inf t^5 K0(r0 t) dt
= c r0^-4 int_0^inf u^5 K0(u) du = 16 c 2^4 Gamma(3)^2 = 1024 c, by the Mellin transform
int_0^inf u^(s-1) K0(u) du = 2^(s-2) Gamma(s/2)^2 (s > 0). The Taylor expansion a_c = c (1 - 4 r^2 + 16 r^4 - ...)
with Delta^2 r^4 = 64 gives the same value. The tail formula is the same computation on |zeta| > v.
(iii) Plancherel and polar coordinates give (2 pi)^-2 int |zeta|^(2m) |a_c^|^2 = 2 pi c^2 r0^4 int_0^inf
t^(2m+1) K0(r0 t)^2 dt, and u = r0 t gives the first form. The second uses the Mellin transform
int_0^inf u^(s-1) K0(u)^2 du = pi^(1/2) Gamma(s/2)^3/(4 Gamma((s + 1)/2)) at s = 2m + 2. For m = 4, 5, 6 the closed
form agrees to ten digits with radial quadrature of |Delta^k a_c|^2 (m = 2k) and |grad Delta^k a_c|^2 (m = 2k + 1)
(FLOAT, research/near_core/core_energy.py).
(iv) a^ = a_c^ + a_r^, so |a^| <= a_c^ + |a_r^| pointwise; integrate against the nonnegative weights |zeta|^4 and
|zeta|^p 1_(|zeta| > v) and use (ii). QED.

Remarks.
1. Values (FLOAT, research/near_core/core_energy.out, core_panels.out): D4(a_c) = 392.718, D5(a_c) = 3744.42,
   D6(a_c) = 43170.3; half of the order-6 Laplacian energy int |Delta^3 a_c|^2 lies in r <= 0.078 and 90 percent in
   r <= 0.28 (confirmed, AT-1329); for the Cartesian tensor density |D^6 a_c|^2, whose total is the same, the
   fractions within those radii are below 0.161 and 0.720 (the second theory agent's notes 17:00Z; AT-1349), and half of it lies within
   r 0.21006 (AT-1348). The 48 contiguous owner panel records of the near cover
   on 1/4096 <= r <= 0.00106 (unaudited) agree with the energies of a_c to 1.3e-6 relative at order 6.
2. The tail proxy of every HF1-NEAR price since theory_v10 (research/hf1_near/hf1_price_e8.py, AT-778) is exactly
   T_q(a_c; v) of (ii) with q = 21/10 (AT-1328). So the proxy was the core's tail, and the part it omitted is at most
   T_q(a_r), a_r = a_n + a_c (Remark 5).
3. Loss of NR-25 (i) on the core (FLOAT, research/kernel_n6/runs/n6s_core.out): T_q(a_c; 20.5) = 0.18716 against
   the m = 6 bound 1.123 with D6(a_c), and 0.2902 for the best m <= 16 (m = 11); at v = 48, 1.58e-6 against 0.0952
   (m = 6) and 1.65e-5 (m = 16). With D_m(a_c) in place of D_m(a_n) the unsplit m = 6 budget of theory_v13 V13-4
   (b) 4 fails at every grid mu with P/D <= 1 (HEURISTIC; reproduced, AT-1331).
4. Use (HEURISTIC; theory_v13 V13-4 (b) 4): with (iv) the HF1-NEAR budget at a grid mu holds if
   A4c (1024 c + M4^+(a_r))/mu^4 + tail_B(a_c) + c_6(mu) D6(a_r) <= B(mu). At erflog 8, rho 1, b0 0.55 this allows
   D6(a_r) up to 4690 at mu 41 with M4^+(a_r) = 0 and 3262 with M4^+(a_r) = 1000. The certified inputs would then be
   tail_B(a_c) and 1024 c (explicit) and the energies of a_r, which the present near-cover records of a_n do not give.
   The core terms are FLOAT quadratures here; a certificate needs a certified K0 tail integral (AT-1328).
5. The sign of the core (added after AT-1328; the origin jet CONFIRMED, AT-1363). a_n(0) = -c: a_tilde =
   (4 u.X - c)/(1 + 4|X|^2) and 1 - cut = 1 at the origin (the numerical auditor research/source013_near_hypotheses/PROOF.md), and
   the exact origin polynomial of a_n (the numerical auditor research/source013_near_endpoints/verification/origin/primary.json) has
   constant term -c and radial part 4 c |x|^2 at order 2, as -a_c has (its order-2 remainder is k (x^2 - y^2) with
   k = 4(c - 1) = 1824676646553933/562949953421312, EXACT, AT-1363, which Delta^2 and Delta^3 annihilate). Apply (iv)
   to a = -a_n. Since M4, T_p and ||D^m .|| are unchanged under a -> -a, this gives M4(a_n) <= 1024 c + M4(a_n + a_c)
   and T_p(a_n; v) <= T_p(a_c; v) + T_p(a_n + a_c; v). So the remainder of Remark 4 and of theory_v13 is
   a_r = a_n + a_c, not a_n - a_c (the first theory agent's note 50a). The center-1 bounds of AT-1329 use only the square roots of the
   two regional energies, so they hold for a_n + a_c as well.
6. The radial identity (the numerical auditor, CONFIRMED, AT-1344 to AT-1346; cited, not a result of this file). The angular mean of
   a_n is -b a_c, with b the radial near cut, so a_n + b a_c has zero angular mean and is orthogonal to every radial
   function in each Laplacian energy on a concentric region; hence on every concentric region and on the whole plane
   E_m(a_n + a_c) = E_m(a_n) - E_m(b a_c) + E_m((1 - b) a_c). With FLOAT radial energies
   (research/near_core/radial_split2.py; its quadrature of E_m(a_c) agrees with (iii) to 2e-16) E6(b a_c) = 1.863223e9
   and E6((1 - b) a_c) = 1046.1 on the whole plane, so the room 4690 of Remark 4 is D6(a_n) <= 43419 against D6(b a_c)
   = 43165, and on the center-1 panel the lower ends of the a_n energies (AT-1215) give E6(a_n + a_c) about 1.3313e5
   there (FLOAT). A certificate needs a certified quadrature of the two radial energies; the first theory agent
   research/near_core/radial_cert.py computes them in ball arithmetic (python-flint acb.integral on t = log r with
   Cauchy-estimate tails); the method is CONFIRMED (AT-1450). Corrected in version 15: its printed lower displays
   were rounded upward from the lower ends of the finite-interval enclosures, and its printed E5 upper is false
   (AT-1451, AT-1452; research/near_core/ERRATA.md). The corrected outward endpoints (CONFIRMED, AT-1453; recomputed
   independently by the numerical auditor, exchange v326) are E6(b a_c) >= 1863222952.88319822 (LB) and E6((1 - b) a_c) <=
   1046.133867963888 (UB), so D6(a_n + a_c)^2 <= D6(a_n)^2 - 1863221906.74933 on the whole plane (CONFIRMED,
   AT-1454); for m = 4 and 5 the differences are 154325.13929406 and 14022950.26400324 (LB). With the second theory agent's Proposition
   NL6, D6(a_n + a_c) >= 45543 on the whole plane (CONFIRMED, AT-1456), this gives D6(a_n) >= 62748 (CONFIRMED,
   AT-1467); both are lower bounds, and no upper of D6(a_n) is certified (AT-1455, AT-1468 OPEN).

## NR-31. T1-Prop-O1-013: the k = 0 lifting at 013 from the matrix component and a weak gradient bound (PROVED as a conditional application: AT-1483, AT-1484)

Status. PROVED as a conditional application: the theory auditor audited the draft of this statement (the first theory agent
exchange_drafts/v14/v14_1b.md, frozen copy in the theory auditor research/o1_013_qualitative_audit/source) and CONFIRMS the
implication (AT-1483) and the cover simplification (AT-1484), with the semigroup transfer in H2 made explicit; the
Proof below retains that step as the theory auditor asks (audit_v450). AT-1485 was OPEN because (H-a) and (H-b) were not certified
for 013 and C_1 is not computed. At version 22 both field premises hold on the whole plane: the theory auditor's actual review of
the numerical auditor's executed complete background union CONFIRMS M >= (704955805507/10^12) I and |grad h| <= 1/4 on R^2 under the
admitted exact-source and containing-arithmetic hypotheses (AT2534 to AT2536; numerical audit exchange v749, v755), so (H-a)
holds with beta = 704955805507/10^12 and (H-b) with G = 1/4. The theory auditor CONFIRMS the resulting application under every
retained analytic hypothesis, first with beta = 3/5 (AT2541, on theory_v21) and then with the whole-plane value: full
k = 0 H^(21/10) regularity with every 0 < beta_1 < 704955805507/10^12, the L2 inverse bound 1/(beta_0 - G) <
0.570726, a finite uncomputed C_1 and no uniform small-k statement (AT2599, on version 22). C_1 is still not
computed, so no numerical invariant ball follows (Remark 3). The statement uses only PROVED frameworks
(FW-C2-O1Hq-v1, AT-031; RC-3 of T2.7, AT-060, AT-123) and CONFIRMED 013 structure facts (AT-674, AT-675). It refines
V11-2 (c) of theory_v11 and reading 2 of V13-5 of theory_v13; it is stated in V14-1 (b) of theory_v14.

Purpose. V11-2 (c) lists for the k = 0 statement at 013 "RC-2, the cover of chain item 3 (BG-3 at beta_* >= 3/5)
and BG-2 with beta_0*". The cover is planned with the joint target (beta, g, S) = (3/5, 1/4, 3/2), and on one angular
leaf of the annulus the sufficient gradient test 1/4 fails (AT-1249). This result records which cover outputs the
qualitative statement needs: the matrix component with any beta > 0 and any certified gradient bound below the base
L2 gap; no strain component and no target 1/4.

Statement. Let rho_* = rho_bar + h_* be the 013 background (the Candidate 007 base rho_bar with the 013 correction
h_* = -Delta phi), h = h_* the density correction of the numerical auditor's cover contract (the numerical auditor
research/source013_background_cover/PROOF.md), and
    M(X) = (c + 1 + sigma V(X).X/(eta + |X|^2)) I - q Sym DV(X) + A(X),   A = [[rho_y, -rho_x/2], [-rho_x/2, 0]],
with q = 21/10, sigma = 1, eta = 1/4 and the fields of rho_*; so M = -N_q of BG-3 at rho_* with r0 = 1/2. Assume
  (H-a) M(X) >= beta I for all X in R^2, for some rational beta > 0;
  (H-b) sup_X |grad h(X)| <= G for a certified rational G < 2.00215453, where beta_0 > 2.00215453 is the L2 gap of
        the base (BG-2, AN-010);
  (H-c) RC-2 and RC-4 for h_* (the exact-potential class and the normalized origin of 013; AT-674, AT-675).
Then L_* k0 = -F_* has a unique L2 solution k0, W k0 is in H^(21/10) with W = (1 + 4|X|^2)^(1/2),
    N(k0) <= N(F_*)/beta_1 + C_1 ||F_*||_2   for every 0 < beta_1 < min(beta, beta_0 - G),
with C_1 finite (not computed), and <X> k0 is in C^(1,1/10).

Proof. FW-C2-O1Hq-v1 (NR-1, AT-031) needs H1 to H5 for the background, with parameters 2 < q < 3, 0 <= sigma <
1 + gamma_* and r0 > 0; q = 21/10, sigma = 1 and r0 = 1/2 qualify (the all-order decay of h_*, AT-674, AT-675, keeps
the base rate gamma = 1 - 1/c > 0). H1 (BG-1 at rho_*) is RC-2, which (H-c) gives. H2 (BG-2 at rho_*) holds with
beta_0* = beta_0 - G > 0. On the common generator domain write L_* = A_h + K_* with K_* f = grad h_*.U[f]. The base
part A_h generates a semigroup of norm at most exp(-beta_0 t), and ||U[f]||_2 <= ||f||_2 gives ||K_*|| <= G. The
variation-of-constants inequality
    ||T_*(t) f||_2 <= exp(-beta_0 t) ||f||_2 + G int_0^t exp(-beta_0 (t - s)) ||T_*(s) f||_2 ds
and Gronwall give ||T_*(t)|| <= exp(-(beta_0 - G) t), and the Neumann argument of RC-3 (AT-060, AT-123) gives the
full inverse with norm at most 1/(beta_0 - G) on the same generator and domain. The semigroup bound is what H2
requires; the inverse bound alone would not supply it (the theory auditor made this step explicit, AT-1483). G < 2.00215453 <
beta_0 gives beta_0* > 0. H3 (BG-3 at rho_* with rate beta) is (H-a), since A = -Kmat and so M = -N_q. H4 (BG-4 at
rho_*) is RC-4. H5, N(F_*) < infinity, holds because W F_* is in every H^s (AT-674, AT-675); a certified value is
N(F_*) <= 0.0055546 (AT-1430, AT-1431). The conclusion is that of T1.2 with beta_0 replaced by beta_0 - G and
beta_1 < min(beta, beta_0*), and the Hoelder statement is the embedding of NR-1 for alpha <= q - 2 = 1/10. QED.

Remarks.
1. The strain components of the cover are not used. The gradient target 1/4 is not needed: on the complete annulus
   41/87 <= |X|^2 <= 83/173 the CONFIRMED bound |grad h| <= 73/200 (AT-1247, AT-1248) holds also on the leaf where
   the test 1/4 fails (AT-1249), and each reviewed root gives 71/500. The target 1/4 is needed for beta_0* >= 7/4 in
   Lemma KB (NR-20), that is for the closure, not for this statement. The strain bound stays a premise of the far
   route.
2. The numerical auditor's admission contract asserts a component on the whole plane if it passes on every root, and tests a
   tighter target separately. (H-b) can therefore be supplied by a weak target (for example g = 1, beta_0* > 1.00215)
   or by a leaf maximum of the endpoint squares of the intersected rh and sh intervals, as in the numerical auditor's prepared
   reduction for 009 (the numerical auditor research/global_gradient_refinement/PROOF.md), applied to the reviewed 013 records. Such
   a weaker target needs complete coverage and its own exact reduction and review (AT-1484).
3. Only the qualitative statement and the bound with a symbolic C_1 follow. One admissible form is C_1 =
   beta_1^(-1) (C_(beta - beta_1)/(beta_0 - G - beta_1))^(1/2) with the compact-remainder constant C_(beta - beta_1)
   of T1.2 (the theory auditor research/o1_013_qualitative_audit/PROOF.md); it is not computed, so the certified N(F_*) gives no
   numerical invariant ball (AT-1485).

## NR-32. T1-Lemma-K0TAIL: closed-form upper of the Fourier tails of the radial core (PROVED: AT-1512, AT-1513)

Status. PROVED: the theory auditor CONFIRMS the lemma under the hypotheses of Remark 4 (AT-1512) and the nine tail_B uppers of the
Corollary and of k0_tail.out at mu = 64, 68, 70, 71, 72, 73, 74, 76 and 80 (AT-1513; the theory auditor
research/radial_core_tail_audit, audit_v460, 279 checks at 384 and 640 bits, every display compared as an exact
rational). AT-1514 is OPEN: the radial core alone closes no near estimate. The numerical auditor's AN349 (exchange v349) proves the
same bound independently. It uses only NR-30 (ii) (PROVED, AT-1328) and the integral representation of K0. It
settles requirement (R3) of V14-4 (b) 4 of theory_v14 (the K0 tails of a_c
at the tail points of the mu 72 window); it is stated in V14-1 (d) of theory_v14.

Purpose. The core-split budget of NR-30 at the chain split charges tail_B(a_c) = (4/3)^q T_q(a_c; 3 mu/4) +
(8/mu)^q T_q(a_c; mu/2). NR-30 (ii) gives T_q(a_c; v) as a one-dimensional K0 integral, which so far entered the
budgets as a FLOAT quadrature. A closed-form upper removes the quadrature.

Statement. Let c > 0, r0 = 1/2, a_c(x) = c/(1 + 4|x|^2) on R^2 and T_p(a_c; v) as in NR-30. For p >= 0 and v > 2p + 1,
    T_p(a_c; v) <= U_p(v) = c pi^(1/2) v^(p + 3/2) e^(-v/2) / (2 (v - 2p - 1)).

Proof. For x > 0, K0(x) = int_0^inf exp(-x cosh s) ds. Since cosh s >= 1 + s^2/2,
    K0(x) <= e^(-x) int_0^inf exp(-x s^2/2) ds = e^(-x) (pi/(2x))^(1/2),
so K0(t/2) <= e^(-t/2) (pi/t)^(1/2) for t > 0. By NR-30 (ii), T_p(a_c; v) = (c/4) int_v^inf t^(p+1) K0(t/2) dt <=
(c/4) pi^(1/2) int_v^inf t^a e^(-t/2) dt with a = p + 1/2. For t >= v, log(t/v) <= t/v - 1, so t^a <= v^a
exp(a (t - v)/v), and with s = t - v and 1/2 - a/v = (v - 2a)/(2v) > 0,
    int_v^inf t^a e^(-t/2) dt <= v^a e^(-v/2) int_0^inf exp(-(v - 2a) s/(2v)) ds = 2 v^(a + 1) e^(-v/2)/(v - 2a).
Multiplying, T_p(a_c; v) <= (c/2) pi^(1/2) v^(a+1) e^(-v/2)/(v - 2a), which is U_p(v) since a + 1 = p + 3/2 and
2a = 2p + 1. QED.

Corollary (UB; research/near_tail/k0_tail.py, runs/k0_tail.out; arb at 256 bits, displays rounded upward). At q =
21/10 and c = 4076476460239181/2251799813685248, the tail points of the mu 72 window give T_q(a_c; 54) <= 1.065484e-7,
T_q(a_c; 36) <= 3.177882e-4 and tail_B(a_c) <= 3.344353e-6 (UB; arb balls [1.06548368145e-7 +/- 2.92e-19],
[0.000317788160151 +/- 4.85e-16] and [3.34435220883e-6 +/- 2.97e-18]). The FLOAT exact values (scipy quadrature of
NR-30 (ii)) are 1.056582e-7, 3.12759e-4 and 3.292883e-6, so the ratios are 1.0084, 1.0161 and 1.0156 (FLOAT). The same
file gives the uppers at mu = 64, 68, 70, 71, 73, 74, 76 and 80, for example tail_B(a_c) <= 4.299236e-6 at mu 71 and
<= 2.60271e-6 at mu 73 (UB). The two general forms of U_p (with r0 symbolic and at r0 = 1/2) are evaluated separately
and their balls overlap at every point.

Remarks.
1. The two inequalities lose a factor that tends to 1 as v grows: K0(x) e^x (2x/pi)^(1/2) = 1 - 1/(8x) + O(x^-2),
   a relative loss of about 1/(4v) at x = v/2; the second step loses about 4a/v^2 relative (a = p + 1/2), since
   int_v^inf t^a e^(-t/2) dt = 2 v^a e^(-v/2) (1 + 2a/v + 4a(a - 1)/v^2 + O(v^-3)) and the bound is 2 v^a e^(-v/2)
   (1 - 2a/v)^(-1). At p = 21/10 the two give about 0.82 percent at v = 54 and 1.50 percent at v = 36, against the
   ratios 1.0084 and 1.0161 of the Corollary (FLOAT; the expansions are asymptotic, not bounds).
2. In the m = 6 core-split budget at mu 72 the core term tail_B(a_c) is about 2.3e-5 of B(mu) = 0.14812 (FLOAT), so
   the excess 5.15e-8 of the upper 3.344353e-6 over the FLOAT exact value 3.292883e-6 is below 1e-5 of the
   margin +0.0063 (FLOAT; HEURISTIC for the margin).
3. The bound holds for every p >= 0 and v > 2p + 1, so it also covers the other tail points of NR-25 (v = 3 mu/4 and
   mu/2 for mu > 4p + 2).
4. Hypotheses (AT-1512). The theory auditor proves the Bessel-integral step in a generic form: for c > 0, r0 > 0, p >= -1/2 and
   v > 0 with r0 v > p + 1/2,
       c r0^2 int_v^inf t^(p+1) K0(r0 t) dt <= c r0^(3/2) (pi/2)^(1/2) v^(p+1/2) e^(-r0 v)/(r0 - (p + 1/2)/v);
   at r0 = 1/2 the condition r0 v > p + 1/2 is v > 2p + 1 and the right side is U_p(v). The Fourier application uses
   the identity of NR-30 (ii), which holds for p >= 0 and r0 = 1/2 with the inverse factor (2 pi)^-2, and the wider
   range p >= -1/2 of the generic step does not extend that identity (AT-1512; numerical audit exchange v349). No claim is made
   at v = 2p + 1.

## NR-33. T1-Lemma-TISFG: a Fourier-moment bound of the T_IS form (PROVED: AT2437)

Status. PROVED: the theory auditor CONFIRMS the lemma with the stated constant under the smoothness and decay, Lemma KER identity
and finite Fourier-moment hypotheses (AT2437; the theory auditor research/h4rest_plan_v01_audit, audit_v746, 772 checks with 625
exact Peetre regressions). AT2441 REFUTES reading the rounded level 0.012125762 of the first statement as equivalent
or necessary; Corollary (b) gives the exact threshold. At 013 the free-gain test of Corollary (b) fails (AT2456 to
AT2458 CONFIRMED). The theory auditor's audit of version 22 CONFIRMS the lemma, the exact threshold
3413098628099173/281474976710656000 and the failure of the unsplit test at 013 within the KER and finite ordinary
Fourier-moment hypotheses (AT2600, audit_v789). Remark 4 (version 24) records the larger lower M_S >=
10529.58564315332 from the numerical auditor's seventh-order origin jets (AT2622 to AT2624, audit_v794; AT2625 OPEN). That the lemma
or this failure settles the actual remainder, an inverse or existence is OPEN (AT2442, AT2459). First stated in
theory_agent_1/research/h4rest/H4REST_PLAN_v01.md section 3 (CLAIMED there; theory_v21 V21-1 (d)).

Purpose. In the H4 join of the second theory agent's T2_H4_v01 the near remainder D_rem^n contains the form T_IS, whose coefficient is
the strain S = Sym D ubar (H4-REST; theory_v21 V21-1 (b)). Proposition FG (AT-513) leaves an unused free gain (G -
A_far) ||J^(-1) g||^2. The lemma bounds T_IS in the same norm, so that T_IS can be paid from that gain, with no rate
and no price, if one Fourier moment of S is small enough.
